### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

investment themes that are likely to dominate price action in 2027. More detailed 
outlooks for the individual asset classes are forthcoming.

Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 3

Markets Outlook 2027: Growing Pains

1. A maturing market cycle 
Stable growth, declining inflation make for a friendly macro backdrop. n
But risks are to the downside, and an ageing market cycle is facing more headwinds. n
Higher rates, wider spreads, and a peaking AI impulse mean the broad asset picture n
is more two-sided ... 
... and argues for more diversified allocations, including bonds and hedges. n
Our headline macro forecasts for 2027 are quite friendly: stable growth, declining 
inflation and peaking policy rates. But as the economic cycle extends, the market cycle is 
increasingly showing its age. For 2026, our view was that even though asset market 
valuations were running ahead of the macro, a positive cyclical backdrop including 
strong investment growth should dominate. While the Iran war has upended many 
forecasts, even without the energy shock the market is running into more conventional 
late-cycle headwinds. Real rates are higher, credit spreads are wider and volatility is 
picking up, especially under the surface. On top of this, the AI impulse, which has been a 
key support to markets through the shocks this year, looks poised to peak in the coming 
year. 
That is likely to prove a bigger headwind to the profit and asset market picture than 
growth, given its larger role in supporting equity markets. Markets have already moved 
some way towards reflecting that the extraordinary surge in profitability in the AI 
infrastructure complex is unlikely to be extrapolated. So a flattening in the gains there 
may be offset by gains further down the waterfall among AI users or other cyclical parts 
of the market, especially if energy prices and rates fall more. But that is a tricky 
balancing act, and further boosts to equity prices are likely to come alongside a higher 
base level of volatility. There are also risks that AI-related drawdowns tighten financial 
conditions more meaningfully, or that energy and policy relief do not arrive on time. 
That leaves the broader risk-asset picture looking more two-sided than it has for several 
years, arguing for more diversified and better-hedged portfolios. 
12 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 4

2. The AI investment impulse peaks 
The AI investment impulse peak is a bigger test for markets than the economy. n
That impulse has been a larger contributor to growth in market value and earnings n
than in GDP. 
Markets have already de-rated AI infrastructure beneficiaries. n
Valuation excesses are less visible, so the focus is on sustainability of record profits. n
Coming into 2026, we expected continued acceleration in AI investment to dominate 
valuation concerns in equities, but likely amid higher volatility and wider credit spreads.  
Although we expect solid AI investment growth to continue, our forecasts imply that the 
investment growth impulse—additional AI investment as a share of GDP—will peak in 
early 2027. From a macro perspective, this should be a manageable event. But it may be 
a bigger test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger 
contributor to growth in earnings and market value than to growth in GDP. Second, 
AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity gains, especially 
companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have made them more 
vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the point at which 
growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters, even if underlying 
growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through that peak allows 
markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and, as growth 
decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as we may 
be starting to see for the AI capex cycle. 
Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant de-rating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are 
most sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. Moreover, robust profits, and a rising share of

valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this cycle is 
whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in profits, 
which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove persistent, 
especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation allowed during that 
tech cycle—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure 
on the cost of capital.

3. Passing—or dropping—the baton 
Strong levels of AI investment growth and profitability should limit the pressure. n
And the baton may be passed from AI infrastructure to other market areas. n
But we see risks of a larger aggregate shock if the market looks forward, or other n
areas cannot take up the slack. 
Tighter financial conditions or credit conditions could create a feedback loop. n
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition through the peak of 
the investment cycle will prove to be. The first is that although the investment impulse 
from AI looks poised to peak, growth in both AI investment and earnings should remain 
firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth 
to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a still-robust 11% in 2027. This may ultimately help to 
limit the de-rating of even the most directly affected areas and should reduce the 
impact on broader growth-sensitive assets, though it should also imply less relief in core 
rates pricing. The second is that the baton may be passed, at least to a degree, from the 
direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies that may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies that see some relief as the crowding out of capital

available to them eases. 
The risk is that what could be more of a rotation—and a flattening in the upward trend in 
asset markets—becomes a bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the 
market begins to look further forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 
on our forecasts); because the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place 
as quickly or as smoothly as needed; or because feedback loops from financial and 
credit conditions accelerate more quickly than in our baseline. We think the risk to 
markets is higher than the risk to the economic picture on each front, because the boom 
itself has had its largest impact there. For that reason, the most likely channel through 
which AI worries could lead to broader economic downside is through financial 
conditions themselves.

4. Energy—balancing risk and risk premium 
An escalation-driven oil spike remains one of the largest near-term risks to markets. n
Our baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices, but the hedge value of energy n
is still high. 
Downside tail to energy prices with clearer/faster resolution. n
Risk premium has fluctuated, so hedge value also varies over time. n
A potential oil price spike driven by another escalation in the war with Iran remains one 
of the biggest near-term risks to the markets and growth picture. Particularly after the 
fall in oil prices so far in November, pricing is vulnerable to any broadening in the conflict 
and new supply disruptions. We would expect that to hurt equities, support the Dollar 
against European currencies and put upward pressure on rates, all else equal. Europe, 
too, faces a precarious winter, given low storage levels in natural gas which leave the 
region vulnerable to risks from both supply and weather. If those risks are avoided, our 
baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices over the coming year even without full 
resolution of the conflict. Despite the ongoing war with Iran, oil market supply has 
largely been restored (even if refining capacity is limited), but risks of escalation and 
further disruptions have generally led the market to demand a fluctuating but 
meaningful risk premium above where we think the fundamental supply-demand picture 
would leave them.

### Page 7

Our baseline is a gradual recovery in supply and reduction in energy prices, leaving 
energy and core commodity returns more muted. But the risk of more serious 
disruptions is real, and energy prices could move significantly higher in those scenarios 
at a time when equities and, potentially, bonds could both be under pressure. The 
hedging value of the asset class remains high for that reason. The wrinkle is that the risk 
premium in oil has varied substantially and conditions the value of the hedge. When the 
risk premium falls, as it has done more recently, that hedging value is likely to be higher. 
When that risk premium is very high, as it was at the end of October, oil markets are 
more vulnerable to downside tails if news flow is better than expected, and outright 
longs (rather than options) have generally been more risky. The downside tail from faster 
progress towards resolution than we expect (not just in Iran, but in Ukraine too) is one of 
the clearest sources of upside risks for broader markets. In that scenario, energy prices 
could come down more quickly, supporting both bonds and equities.

5. Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value 
Rates moved up in 2026, on oil, hikes, AI spending and fiscal deficits. n
Central bank pricing now too hawkish relative to our baseline. n
Bonds should be a better hedge as supply shocks give way to demand stresses. n
Higher running yield, more paths to relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher n
bond allocations. 
Interest rates moved higher in 2026 after a low point on the eve of the Iran war. The 
resultant surge in energy prices and central bank pricing has been the primary catalyst 
across most DM and EM rates markets, but real rates, especially in the US, have 
continued to move higher as investment demand from the AI build-out interacts with 
sustained fiscal deficits. Despite that backdrop, we think that central bank pricing in 
many economies, including the US and the UK, is too hawkish relative to what is likely to 
be delivered. Even with the energy shock, the underlying inflation story is still fairly 
benign, which means that policy rates are likely to peak at lower levels relative to 
2023-2024 and reverse earlier. The risk to this view is that the energy shock extends 
further or inflation remains stickier and the labour market proves more resilient, as it did 
this year. 
Bonds have been a poor hedge in this environment of energy supply shocks, rate hikes

and real rate pressures from private and public sector spending. But we think the case 
for duration has shifted in a more constructive direction for several reasons. First, on a 
static basis, bond yields are now providing a good running yield and a decent spread to 
cash. Second, valuations are cheap (we see UST 10y yields at 4.6% by end-2027), 
positioning is underweight, and there are paths to some shallow rate relief from current 
levels under our benign baseline scenario, and scope for larger moves if AI spending 
decelerates more quickly. Third, the increase in yields has started to create stresses that 
indicate that a higher level of real rates may be difficult to sustain. Bonds are already 
starting to illustrate their value as a hedge against shocks of these types—with Bunds 
rallying as French sovereign spreads widened and USTs rallying as tech stocks came 
under pressure. Although energy spikes remain a near-term risk, we are more positive 
about bond allocations even after the recent rally, in part because of that hedging value.

6. A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies 
After tight ranges in 2026, lower inflation, energy prices make for a softer Dollar. n
But a bigger USD swing could come if US tech exceptionalism erodes. n
JPY is a more attractive hedge with ‘team Japan’ focused on limiting Yen weakness. n
CNY appreciation has further to run given undervaluation and a surging trade n
surplus.  
The broad Dollar is finishing 2026 on the strong side of tight ranges, buoyed by a sturdy 
US nominal growth backdrop, higher energy prices, and a shift to more hawkish Fed 
pricing. Under our baseline forecast, all three impulses should fade: alongside trend 
growth we expect inflation in the US to converge towards target, energy prices should 
decline as the Middle East disruptions gradually normalize and we expect the Fed to 
move from hikes to cuts as we head into late 2027 and early 2028. That should make for

a softer Dollar backdrop, and we have pencilled in a roughly 3% fall in the broad 
trade-weighted Dollar in 2027. 
But the real swing factor for FX is likely to be how the peak in AI-related tech spending 
affects US equity returns. Over the past several years, tech-related macro and market 
outperformance in the US has sucked in equity capital flows from the rest of the world 
and has been the primary factor supporting the Dollar’s premium valuation. Our 
baseline forecasts envisage a more even distribution of returns globally rather than US 
underperformance. But if US tech performance erodes meaningfully, that would likely 
come alongside some cyclical underperformance of the Dollar rather than a traditional 
‘safe haven’ bid, as we have already seen in short periods over the past couple of years. 
Instead, we would expect the undervalued Yen to prove a better haven in that scenario, 
given its typical sensitivity to equities and rates falling together. With Japanese policy 
authorities also now focused on limiting further Yen depreciation and encouraging 
inflows into Japanese assets, we think that the Yen should increasingly be a more 
attractive hedge to pro-cyclical portfolios. We also see scope for the Yuan to continue to 
appreciate gradually as China’s trade surplus keeps growing, and forward markets 
underestimate how far this process can run given the deep undervaluation starting 
point. While domestic demand is also very weak, we have argued that China needs both 
currency appreciation and expansionary policy, two instruments to hit two targets, to 
move towards better external and internal balance.

European markets have been under pressure from energy ToT, higher global yields n
and sovereign stresses. 
Even if global shocks abate, local concerns still persist until the French election, n
keeping EUR downside attractive.

### Page 10

UK equities are resilient and defensive, Sterling has continued to strengthen. n
UK Gilts may be clearer outperformers given their highest beta to oil prices. n
European macro markets have had a tough year, primarily reflecting a negative terms of
trade shock, rate tightening without an offsetting investment spending boom, and most
recently the reappearance of sovereign stresses, especially in France. Even with the
repricing, there are still challenges: the energy risks are likely to be most acute going

through the winter, and the underlying fiscal issues in France are unlikely to be
adequately addressed until the French election in the Spring of 2027. As a result, using
the Euro as a funder for other crosses has been a popular position, and the skew of risks
and low carry continue to make downside optionality attractive for the near term. While
Europe remains hostage to global shocks, the main paths to relief also run through those
global channels—energy relief would be most helpful, as would lower yields in the rest of

core markets. Together they would also lessen the immediate pressure on fiscal budgets,
even though the underlying primary deficit would still need to be addressed. Any
slowdown in AI-related spending may also show European assets in a better relative light
given a much smaller share of tech in the region’s equity indices.
In those scenarios, the UK may shine even brighter. Gilts have had the highest beta to oil
prices, so a reduction in those pressures may provide the most relief to UK rates and to

the fiscal picture there. UK equity indices also have less tech exposure than the US
(although they do embed significant energy exposure), and so may also prove more
defensive in an AI-related spending slowdown and any associated market pressures. And
while the currency may also benefit from a softer Dollar backdrop, it is already the most
overvalued currency within our G10 coverage. Much has gone right for the Pound in
recent months—a smooth transition to a new Labour government, a ‘do-no-harm’

budget delivery, the BoE shifting to hikes, and encouraging talk of a closer settlement
with the EU. But we think further gains are likely to be more challenging, with our
monetary policy forecasts most dovish to market pricing in the UK.
12 November 2026   10
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 11

8. The stress of financing the boom 
AI investment financing has driven credit and equity issuance to cycle records. n
This has been digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit that has n
led spreads wider. 
A further widening in US spreads looks likely and a higher chance of meaningful n
widening than meaningful tightening. 
Europe offers more risk premium; mortgages and structured credit give some refuge n
from corporate supply. 
Credit and equity issuance has been hitting fresh cycle records, and the financing needs 
of the AI investment boom mean that those needs are likely to build further in 2027. So 
far, that issuance has—for the most part—been digestible, albeit with discounted 
pricing, and the credit underperformance that we expected in 2026 because of these 
dynamics has only really become clear in the last few months. Balance sheets of the 
major borrowers are strong and while free cash flow positions for the hyperscalers have 
deteriorated sharply, the overall corporate sector is much more balanced. We expect 
continued bifurcation between AI-related issuers and “everything else”, with episodic 
indigestion of jumbo AI debt financing leading to wider spreads for AI-related sectors.  
We expect further widening in index-level credit spreads in 2027, though in our base 
case the scope is fairly modest. The risks around that view are skewed though. It is hard 
to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is more scope for 
tightening in Europe where the risk premium has increased more), and easier to see how 
spreads could move wider, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate more than we 
expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability of the AI 
investment boom. We expect modest US HY underperformance (decompression) in our

baseline, but the risk is that problems in the left tail of weaker borrowers create more 
turbulent conditions for broader financing. Mortgages and structured credit markets 
may offer some refuge from the heavy corporate supply. From 1998 to 2000, there was 
sustained widening in credit spreads and substantial decoupling between credit and 
equity markets. Without the large capital inflows and goods price deflation from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998, we think that kind of divergence will be harder now.

9. EM—resilience and diversification 
2026 is one more year when EM assets have shown resilience/outperformance in the n
face of external shocks. 
But the flip side is less compelling valuations and lower carry, despite friendly macro. n
So the case for EM investing is exposure to high, diversified and resilient yields in EM n
fixed income ... 
... and a regional and sectorally diversified earnings mix in EM equities.  n
EM assets have again demonstrated impressive resilience in 2026 in the face of shocks 
from high energy prices and the spikes in core rates. EM rates have outperformed core 
rates, EM FX total returns are outpacing G9 and EM equities are on track for a narrower 
but still double-digit year after 30% gains in 2025. The upshot of this resilience and 
outperformance is that in many places valuations are less compelling than before. EM 
local rate spreads are tight relative to core rates, EM credit spreads are the tightest in 
years despite attractive all-in yields, and EM FX carry and valuations are firmly 
middle-of-the-road. 
Even though the baseline macro backdrop looks friendly for EM investing and 
carry-harvesting in 2027, less attractive valuations and carry levels mean that it is about 
gaining exposure to high diversified yields in both hard and local currency fixed income. 
In many cases, such as South Africa, Hungary, Colombia and India, those higher yields 
come with improving fiscal and structural reform stories. Likewise, in the case of EM 
equities, while the major markets are not expensive, much of the value in the headline 
index is an artefact of the surge in AI earnings that will be hard to repeat. Rather, the 
case for EM equity length is also about gaining exposure to a diversified earnings mix.

### Page 13

The EM equity index, especially the less concentrated, equal-weighted flavours, provide 
exposure not just to the tech and AI complex in North Asia, but also to cyclical and 
commodity-related growth in South Africa and LatAm, and to larger domestic demand 
stories such as India. And if we see a larger move lower in core rates or the Dollar than 
our baseline expectations, EM local assets provide upside convexity that is harder to 
replicate elsewhere.

10. Growth risks and tail hedges 
A benign baseline, but growth risks now skew to the downside. n
Diversification benefits from bonds, commodities and equity selection. n
Equity index volatility has scope to rise further. n
Downside in rates, USD/JPY, upside in energy and protection in equity options. n
With a steady economic backdrop, our baseline forecasts for 2027 are for positive 
returns across equities and bonds. But with a maturing market cycle and significant 
uncertainty around core drivers of the outlook, we continue to emphasize the value of 
diversification and hedges to protect against key risks. Some of those risks—from higher 
oil prices or sticky core inflation—are likely to push rates higher. But a growing number—
a sharper reversal in the AI boom, cybersecurity fears, AI-related labour-shedding or 
financial stresses—would push both growth and rate expectations lower. The further we 
move into 2027, the more likely it is that the key risks are positive for bonds, as the 
threats from central bank hikes, energy price spikes and the AI investment tailwind 
recede. Diversification remains valuable in this environment. As we have said, the value 
of bonds as a diversifying asset is returning as the skew of risks shifts. And 
commodities—especially energy—continue to play a hedging role for portfolios, 
particularly when implied risk premia fall as they have recently.  
Alongside diversification, direct hedges are worth considering. Positioning for lower 
rates in the US and Japan, or for jointly lower rates and equities are potential ways to 
protect against deeper downside growth tails. In FX, USD/JPY downside may play a 
similar role, given that it generally performs well when rates and equities are both falling

broadly. For near-term energy upside, hedges in European gas futures and TTF call

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank Negara Malaysia (BNM) (5 
November)

As written in our Macro Strategy note, 
BNM’s final meeting of 2026 on 5 
November will be live and a close call, 
after the central bank opened the door to 
monetary policy normalisation during its 
September review. BNM Governor Abdul 
Rasheed Ghaffour reiterated in a 
Bloomberg interview on 15 October that 
the central bank has space to adjust 
policy if needed, as policymakers are 
vigilant about upside inflationary 
pressures amid strong economic growth. 
He also said that policy decisions will be 
data dependent. We think the latest 
available September data for the 
upcoming meeting suggest that 
policymakers can afford to be patient 
until 1H27, maintaining the Overnight 
Policy Rate at 2.75% for now. Inflation 
remained contained within the lower half 
of the official 2026 inflation forecast 
range of 1.5-2.5%, notwithstanding 
external price pressures and robust 
economic activity. Advance estimates 
showed GDP growth of 5.3% yoy in 3Q26, 
while exports jumped to a five-year high 
of 54.5% yoy in September, 
supercharged by the artificial 
intelligence boom. However, headline 
inflation eased slightly to 1.8% yoy in 
September 2026 from 1.9% yoy in August, 
while core inflation remained stable at 1.7% 
yoy. Fuel subsidies have limited the pass-
through of upstream cost pressures to 
consumer prices, alongside stable 
domestic demand conditions.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

South Korea

October trade and inflation data are due 
for release. Export growth is expected to 
accelerate to nearly 100% yoy, up from 
83.7% in September, while the trade 
surplus is forecast to remain above 
USD40bn for another month. CPI 
inflation is expected to hold steady at 2.9% 
yoy. Overall, the data should reinforce 
the view that AI- and semiconductor-
driven growth and the expansion in 
South Korea’s trade surplus remain 
firmly on track, providing fundamental 
support for the KOSPI and KRW. 
Meanwhile, inflation remains sticky 
above the BOK’s 2% target, bolstering 
the case for further monetary tightening 
as early as the November policy meeting.

Taiwan

October inflation data are forthcoming. 
Headline CPI inflation is expected to 
remain above 2% for the sixth 
consecutive month, at 2.3% yoy. Core 
CPI inflation is also likely to stay above 2% 
for the sixth consecutive month, at 2.3% 
yoy. Despite government subsidies on 
fuel and electricity prices, higher global 
energy prices continue to feed through 
to CPI via items such as airfares and tour 
package fees. Services inflation also 
remains sticky, particularly in food away 
from home, housing, transportation, and 
entertainment. Meanwhile, domestic 
inflation expectations appear to be 
gradually rising. A recent private-sector 
survey showed that the public expects

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

inflation to average 2.5% in 2026, with 
52% of respondents expecting inflation 
to exceed 2.5%. Against this backdrop, 
we maintain our view that the central 
bank will resume rate hikes in December, 
raising the policy rate by 12.5 bps to 
2.125%.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to rise to 3.2% yoy in October 2026, 
extending the increase over the previous 
two months, and moving slightly above 
the upper end of the Bank of Thailand’s 
1-3% target range. This was likely driven 
by faster energy inflation amid an 
uptrend in diesel prices, alongside a 
rebound in food inflation. The inflation 
uptrend was likely broadly in line with 
the central bank's expectations and 
mainly supply-side driven, supporting its 
decision to look through the temporary 
price pressures and keep its policy rate 
unchanged at its October meeting.

Vietnam

We expect Vietnam’s October 2026 
goods exports and retail sales to remain 
robust at 42.0% yoy and 14.0% yoy year-
to-date, respectively, while headline 
inflation is likely to stay elevated at 
around 5.0% yoy. Exports have 
continued to be supported by strong 
electronics and machinery shipments 
amid a favourable technology cycle with 
spillovers from robust artificial 
intelligence-related demand. Retail 
sales were likely bolstered by firm 
household spending, supported by 
favourable labour market conditions, 
and continued strength in tourism. 
Headline inflation has remained elevated 
and above the central bank’s target of 
4.5% for the year, driven by price 
pressures from transport and energy, 
food, as well as material costs. This 
poses a challenge for policymakers as 
they seek to balance inflation risks 
against ambitions of achieving at least 10% 
economic growth this year.

Hong Kong

Hong Kong’s economy is expected to 
decelerate from 4.3% yoy in Q2 to 2.6% 
in Q3, largely due to slower private 
consumption. Despite a 13% increase in 
Mainland visitor arrivals during the 
period, domestic consumption continued 
to be weighed down by a weakening 
labour market, with the unemployment 
rate elevated at 3.9% in September. 
Meanwhile, higher interest rates have 
dampened investment sentiment and 
capital market activity. The Centa-City 
Leading Index remained broadly flat, 
while the Hang Seng Index was range-
bound at around 25,000. The positive 
wealth effect seen in 1H has faded as 
asset market performance remained 
muted. A strong HKD continued to 
encourage outbound tourism, evidenced 
by an increase in average daily resident 
departures from 6.9% yoy in Q2 to 10.9% 
in Q3.

However, GDP growth is expected to be 
buffered by improving net exports amid 
the ongoing AI-electronics upcycle. 
Export growth picked up and outpaced 
import growth during Q3. Hong Kong, as 
China’s free port and re-export hub, is 
benefiting from stronger demand for 
Chinese electronic goods.

Indonesia/ Philippines

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Indonesia’s 3Q GDP likely moderated to 
5.2% y/y. Soft consumer confidence and 
retail sales growth point to weaker 
consumption, compounded by a gradual 
rise in inflation. Fiscal measures likely 
helped spending among low-income 
households. Despite the quarterly 
moderation, we maintain our full-year 
growth forecast at 5.3% y/y.

Inflation rates in Indonesia and the 
Philippines are expected to rise, albeit to 
different degrees. Indonesia’s inflation is 
on course to edge up modestly, while in 
the Philippines, stronger pass-through 
from higher oil prices, food and 
transportation costs, as well as imported 
inflation pressures stemming from a 
weaker peso, is likely to push inflation to 
a multi-year high.

Executive summary
在經過 9-10 ⽉全球半導體及科技股估值超過 30% 的技術修正以來，投資⼈開

公司。

Document 439: When tokenization meets securitized debt markets (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/When tokenization meets securitized debt markets (2026-11-17).pdf

Deutsche Bank Research Institute

### Page 2

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
When tokenization meets securitized debt markets
Asset tokenization continues to grow in 2026. Excluding stablecoins, the
market grew from ~$10bn in Jan 2025 to ~$40bn in Nov 2026. Including
tokens confined to closed or permissioned environments, the total reached
~$420bn (here).
This chartbook explores tokenization’s potential in securitized markets, where

complex structuring, long settlement times and high transaction costs make
the asset class a strong candidate for tokenization.
Today, tokenization mainly improves securities-market infrastructure by
streamlining intermediation, cutting loan origination costs and speeding up
institutional securitization. US-based companies Figure Technologies,
Centrifuge and Maple Finance illustrate the benefits.
The transformative end state combines tokenized infrastructure (represented

tokenization) with tokens transferable outside the issuing platform (distributed
tokenization). Only when both are combined could tokenization reduce credit
risk, deepen the investor base, speed up the flow of capital, and lead to a more
robust, liquid US securitized market.
In this chartbook, we: (i) explain securitization and its challenges; (ii) examine
tokenization’s potential benefits; and (iii) outline the steps needed for
widespread adoption.

….but only ~$8bn in distributed value.
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data as of Nov 12, 2026. Credit combines asset-backed credit,
corporate credit, diversified credit and specialty finance. “Represented” refers to tokens using the blockchain
as a recordkeeping layer, enhancing transparency, reconciliation, and operational efficiency without enabling
onchain investor transfer or distribution.
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data as of Nov 13, 2026. “Distributed”

refers to tokens transferable outside the issuing platform and between wallets; it does
not measure trading activity.
Tokenized credit’s total value is ~$50bn in November, with Figure’s HELOC
token representing $26trn…

### Page 3

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
A ~$15trn market financing housing, consumers and companies
❑ The securitized (structured credit) market includes residential real estate
(RMBS), commercial real estate (CMBS), consumer borrowing (ABS –
autos, credit cards, student loans), and corporate debt (CLOs).
❑ It is a material part of the US growth engine: ~$15trn of debt outstanding,
roughly half of 2025 US nominal GDP. About 75% is linked to US housing

market.
❑ This market emerged in the late 1970s, when the US economy struggled
with sluggish growth and high inflation from elevated energy costs.
❑ Securitization was seen as financial innovation: It let bank lenders move
debt off their balance sheets and syndicate it to public markets as a
pooled asset.
❑ In the three decades before the GFC, the market’s growth increased
capital velocity in US debt markets while simultaneously reducing
financing costs for both borrowers and originators.

❑ Securitization produces pricing efficiency by bundling a diversified pool
of assets with consistent cashflows (e.g., such as corporate loans or
monthly mortgage payments) into a security held by a bankruptcy remote
special purpose vehicle (SPV).
❑ These pooled assets are then sliced into “tranches” with varying credit
risk and corresponding levels of yield, sold to institutional investors (life
insurers to hedge funds) that find relative value in the structure and

diversification.
Residential mortgages (RMBS) plays an outsized role in the market
The US Securitized market is approaching $16trn in market size
Sources: SIFMA, Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
Sources: SIFMA, US Federal Reserve, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 4

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Securitization is held back by complexity, slow settlement and high costs
A CLO investor can expect their first coupon payment typically 6 months
after a deal’s pricing date
Although securitization is deeply embedded into the US financial
system, several challenges remain:
❑ Inherent complexity: Collateralization complicates ownership
tracking, especially as deals are upsized, widely distributed and

traded in a secondary market. This was visible during the GFC, and in
recent pockets of credit stress – the tracking of collateral and of
asset ownership can present logistical hurdles over the lifetime of an
asset.
❑ Long settlement times: Complex structuring can delay settlement
and prolong the liquidity gap between allocating capital to a deal and
when the first coupon distribution takes place.  For example, a new
issue CLO can take up to two months from pricing to close, with an

additional four months before the first coupon distribution date takes
place.
❑ High transaction costs: Intermediaries between the buyer and seller
provide essential services but increase costs when issuing a deal.
Additionally, the ongoing deal administration from monthly collateral
monitoring to coupon distributions adds an additional layer of
logistical complexity to a securitization post close. The key
stakeholders required in this value chain range from arranging banks,

trustees, rating agencies, and lawyers.
Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
4

### Page 5

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
❑ Tokenization records assets or claims as digital tokens on a distributed
ledger (i.e., blockchain), using public or permissioned networks.
❑ Potential benefits: Faster transfers, automated servicing and smaller
investment units.
❑ Shared records can reduce reconciliation, but trading hours, redemption
terms and investor access remain product-specific.
Two issuance models:

❑ On-chain (native): The security or fund share is issued directly on the
distributed ledger/blockchain.
❑ Off-chain (tokenized representation): The underlying asset remains
outside the blockchain, but ownership claims and economic rights are
represented digitally on-chain.
❑ Tokens may be then either transferred to external wallets (distributed) or
platform-bound on the blockchain (represented).
Tokenization can move issuance, settlement and servicing onto a shared ledger

### Page 6

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Credit is tokenized at wrapper or asset level; most value is still represented
There are two broad approaches to tokenizing credit
❑ Wrapper-level tokenization: The underlying credit assets remain in a
conventional portfolio structure, while a fund or SPV is tokenized, e.g.
Galaxy’s new-issue CLO with debt tranches issued and tokenized on
Avalanche, and Apollo Diversified Credit (ACRED).

❑ Asset-level / native tokenization: Blockchain is integrated into the
underlying credit asset's issuance, ownership record and/or lifecycle.
Examples are Figure Technologies and Tradable, which use blockchains to
support loan administration, ownership records, payments tracking, and
servicing.
❑ Figure can be broken down into distributed vs represented, given it
can be reclassified if they become movable and transferrable.
❑ Asset-level vs wrapper-level shows what is tokenized; distributed vs

represented shows how the token can circulate.
State of tokenized securities
❑ Private credit stands ~$50bn in total (~$8bn distributed vs ~$42bn
represented). Includes asset-backed credit ($33bn), specialty finance
($13bn), corporate credit ($2bn), and diversified credit ($795mn).
❑ Use case is mostly record-keeping; growing adoption for transferable
tokenized credit funds.
❑ Tokenized securitized market is also still mostly at the fund level (i.e. KKR &

Securitize, Hamilton Lane) but efforts to tokenize underlying assets is
growing (i.e. Figure, Centrifuge, Intain). E.g. Figure Technologies is
reportedly prepping its first fully tokenized RMBS issuance in the US for
late 2026/early 2027.

Sources: Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 7

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Benefits: Transparency – one shared record replaces three reconciled copies
❑ Problem: Traditional securitization can be opaque. Investors often rely on
periodic, manually reconciled reports rather than direct visibility.
❑ Solution: A shared ledger for every party, tokenization allows investors to
access an audit trail time-stamped with evidence of tamper history, visible

holder register to both issuer and regulator, and loan level by default,
making it permissioned.
Potential use case examples
❑ Immutable, public records of the blockchain prevents double pledging of
assets (e.g., First Brands controversy).
❑ Illuminates circular financing (e.g., AI data centre capex buildout), where a
company provides liquidity to a second company so that it can buy its
goods/services.
❑ Commingled cash flows can obscure a company’s operating

performance and can make it more complex for an investor in
evaluating its underlying economic health and cash position.
❑ Enables more bespoke structures (i.e., multi-strategy CDOs for private
credit loan books), by mapping investor exposure to underlying assets,
helping establish correlations and credit risk that may not be immediately
apparent.

### Page 8

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Benefits: Liquidity – platforms can build a supply-and-demand flywheel
❑ Problem: The structural complexity of the asset class can negatively
impact investor sentiment, serving as a headwind for market growth, and
ultimately impact market depth, liquidity, and prices. And the operational
complexity required for bringing a securitized deal to market can lead to

long settlement times, producing a liquidity gap for an investor.
❑ Solution: The asset transparency resulting from the use of a shared
ledger as core, underlying market infrastructure would help to draw-in
more investors that have traditionally sat on the sidelines. This has been a
hurdle for both structured credit and private credit assets.
❑ A decrease in the perceived complexity cost should naturally lead to a
cohort of net-new investors allocating to the asset class, enabling both

market growth and the added liquidity achieved from a developed
secondary market. An analog, TradFi example of this can be found in the
CLO market, where primary market growth has enabled a robust
secondary market to evolve, further supporting investor sentiment for the
asset class.  The scale achieved has been a factor in driving spreads
tighter over the last few years.
❑ The process simplification enabled by using a block-chain as
foundational infrastructure can lead to time savings, spurring demand

which then attracts greater supply.  Figure Technology’s “Connect”
Platform, a two-sided marketplace developed on the Provenance
blockchain, is an example, connecting loan originators with asset buyers.
Its HELOC offering, which lowers the time and cost required for an
originator to price a loan, has been the service which has played an
outsized role in driving market adoption – Q3 saw growth, with Figure
Connect volume reaching $4.3bn compared to Q2’s $2.8bn.

Connect is an early example of a tokenized securitization marketplace
Sources: Figure Technology Solutions financial filings, AlphaSense,, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 9

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Benefits: Lower costs and faster settlement – ~50–90 bps to bring a deal to market
❑ Problem: Intermediaries (e.g., arranging banks, rating agencies,
lawyers, and trustees) provide essential services for deal activity and
the ongoing administration of cash distributions and collateral
monitoring.
❑ Yet, this costs investors and borrowers fees and processing time,

delaying when an investor receives their first cash distribution by up to
4 months.
❑ Solution: Embedding blockchain into the securitization value chain
could substitute outright (the rails and software providing the service)
or help stakeholders coordinate more efficiently, lowering transaction
costs and time to value for investors. This would also compress
settlement timelines and speed up capital flows.
❑ Net impact: As a distributed ledger, blockchain has features relevant to

securitization: immutable records ensure consistent, reliable data
permeance, and distribution gives participants greater data access.
❑ Embedding blockchain in the securitized value chain would enable four
qualities for the market that would unlock the next stage of growth:
transparency, liquidity, cost efficiency, and speed of settlement.
❑ The savings generated could bring deal costs down materially, by
upwards of 85%.

### Page 10

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Securitization has supported US growth; blockchain could be the next step
The size of the US Securitized market is ~45% of US GDP❑ Structured credit has been an asset class traditionally associated with
financial innovation: it turns a pool of assets that individually carry lower
credit ratings and more credit risk, into a structured pool whose senior
tranche is often AAA.

❑ And it has been an economic growth driver – at $15trn of debt
outstanding, it is roughly 20% of US nonfinancial debt outstanding, and
just north of 45% of US GDP.
❑ The next inflection point of growth for the market could come from using
blockchain technology to optimize the securitization value chain,
embedding origination, structuring, distribution, and administration on-
chain as programmable, automated services.
❑ The continued growth of stablecoins in the years ahead, in addition to US

Treasury funds will be a necessary precursor for the growth of the
tokenized, securitized markets, as it has been for the Structured credit
markets in the TradFi world.
❑ An important relative value feature that a Structured asset provides is the
yield over US government debt – as more investment flows into the DeFi
space, Tokenized Structured products will see material growth.
❑Ultimately, the market’s expansion would be a tailwind for US GDP
growth.

US US SEC stipulates that the tokenized format of credit/structured products does not change how federal securities laws apply to it.
EU
Tokenized securities fall outside MiCA. Falls entirely under EU financial

protection and trading rules).
UK
Digital sandbox, 16 firms are issuing and settling tokenized assets in the
Digital Securities sandbox. A tokenization roadmap is due later this year,
with a custody consultation in H1 2027.
Hong Kong Puts security tokens under the same regulation as traditional securities.
Singapore Project Guardian includes native issuance of asset-backed security and several tokenized private credit funds.
Japan Puts security tokens under the same regulation as traditional securities.

China Banned security tokens on shore, but off-shore tokens can have regulatory approval
❑ The tokenized RWA market remains small ($421bn in Nov 2026)
relative to traditional markets, and token issuance does not
automatically create secondary-market liquidity. Tokenized
securitized debt faces similar challenges to broader tokenized trends.
❑ Secondary-market liquidity remains low. Issuance growth has not
consistently translated into active trading or deep markets.

❑ Regulatory uncertainty: Fragmented or incomplete frameworks slow
institutional adoption and complicate integration with traditional
financial infrastructure. Legal uncertainty persists over whether token
ownership conveys ownership of the underlying asset or a claim on it.
❑ Fragmented infrastructure: Public blockchains, permissioned
institutional ledgers and hybrid platforms often lack interoperability,
fragmenting markets. Clearstream, DTCC and Euroclear published a

joint framework to address this in March 2026.
❑ Limited integration with existing custody, settlement, and record-
keeping arrangements prevents institutions from scaling tokenized
products. Proprietary infrastructure and centralized controls also
raise questions about ownership and governance.

### Page 12

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026 12
Outlook: Tokenization is still infrastructure-led; gains are operational today, credit-risk and liquidity benefits come
later
Tokenized securitization still mostly used for record-keeping purposes; how
much financial activity is adopting blockchain is still small
❑ Tokenization still mostly helps with the infrastructure/plumbing layer of the
securitized market: data, servicing, settlement and collateral mobility.

❑ Hence, distributed value of the tokenized securitized market ($8bn) remains
small compared to represented ($42bn).
❑ In contrast, US Treasury debt remains the largest (ex-stablecoins) on-chain
tokenized asset segment reaching $14bn (distributed) in November 2026.
❑ Demand reflects appetite for liquid, short-term, risk-free collateral, that can
generate yield unlike illiquid segments like private credit.
Potential to transform credit risk, but increased liquidity is further out

❑ Industry is currently in the infrastructure phase. Gains are so far operational:
lower costs, faster settlement times and data transparency.
❑ Move towards tokenizing underlying assets (rather than fund-wrapper style)
has potential to effectively reduce credit risk by countering double-pledging,
settlement risks.
❑ Tokenization remains concentrated among a small number of issuers and lacks
a deep, and liquid secondary market. Distributed value is still small, illustrating

limited activity in actual borrowing demand.
❑ Near term, tokenized Treasuries and money market funds will be the first
market to scale. Longer-term opportunities for private credit, once efforts to
integrate tokenized infrastructure with legacy systems (i.e. DTCC, NYSE,
Nasdaq), money (stablecoins, tokenized deposits), and broader regulation
progresses.

《盤勢看法》
科技類股於10月中旬出現明顯修正，不過市場資金
並未明顯撤離，而是在其他類股輪動，使標普500
指數的修正幅度相對有限。長期來說，穩健的經濟
基本面才是股市行情延續的關鍵，而目前的景氣和
企業獲利支持這樣的趨勢，只是高利率環境仍將使
市場資金更加嚴格看待資產的成長性。拉美方面，
現任左翼總統魯拉在10月初第一輪投票落後的情況
下，於第二輪決選中逆轉，市場重新定價財政前景，
利率上升壓力將提高。據此，將拉美和巴西股市的
評等由正向下調為中立。

《貨幣政策-聯準會偏鷹發言但不急於連續升息》
10月FOMC會議如市場預期維持政策利率在3.75%-
4.0%不變，本次會議的重點可歸納如下：
◼ 聯準會認為美國經濟非常強勁，消費支出健康，
企業投資強勁，通膨仍處於偏高水準，「但也
不急於立刻升息」。華許的解釋並不是通膨風
險已經消失，而是認為先前的政策行動已經透
過金融條件產生緊縮效果，投資人開始更加審
慎地配置資金，部分資產的信用利差擴大。
◼ 縮減資產負債表不是當前政策的優先選項，相
關問題仍交由工作小組研究，這表示雖然他在
長期制度設計上支持重新檢討聯準會資產負債
表的規模與功能，也不代表短期內就會立即推
動更激進的量化緊縮。
雖然華許一如既往地不提供對未來利率路徑的承諾，

但從記者會的發言可大致推論，目前的方案是間隔
一次會議再採取下一個新的行動，本次會議暫停升
息，12月會議將進行下一次升息。2027年1月的會
議按兵不動，3月會議視市場情況再決定。本行的基
準情境是，2027年開始會看到關稅效應、能源衝擊
對通膨的影響逐漸消退，因此不認為2027年3月有
升息的必要性。風險則在於美伊衝突進一步升級，
使企業利用年初例行漲價的機會將成本壓力轉嫁給
消費者，若此，3月可能再升息一次。但本行仍定調
9月以來的升息決策為「風險管理式的升息」，而非
持續性的升息循環。加上華許並未將縮減資產負債
表列入優先事項，過度緊縮的風險不大。
重點摘要
• 本行仍定調9月以來的升息決

《總經情勢和殖利率-景氣穩健但不到過熱程度，美
債壓力有緩和跡象》
美國3Q26 GDP季增年率3.4%，高於2Q26的2.2%，
主要受惠於消費表現優於預期。在油價高漲、利率
上揚的環境當中，美國消費的強勁程度相當出乎意
料。其實個人所得成長率、就業動能，都僅是溫和
成長，整體消費的動能，和股市帶來的財富效應息
息相關。數據顯示，美國家庭持有的股權價值已接
近年稅後所得的3倍。但這也帶來一些疑慮，如k型
消費、景氣對股市的敏感度太高等。綜合來看，美
國的經濟表現不錯，但並不到過熱程度。
美國10年期公債殖利率自7月開始一波上行趨勢，
10月上旬一度升破5.3%，但近期已回落到5%之下，
一部分原因是市場對貨幣政策的共識已經開始凝聚，

以一季進行一次利率調整為原則，目前的經濟數據
尚不足以改變這點；另外原因是如同華許所述，長
率上升、金融條件往收緊的方向，已經透過融資成
本、信用利差、投資人風險偏好來影響資本配置。
當然這並不意味著公債利率將開啟下行趨勢，地緣
風險和財政問題仍不時帶來干擾，不過至少就近期
走勢而言，美債市場面臨的壓力已有初步緩和跡象。
重點摘要
• 美國的經濟表現不錯，但並
不到過熱程度。市場對貨幣
政策的共識已經開始凝聚，
以一季進行一次利率調整為
原則，美債市場面臨的壓力

《美股盤勢-景氣穩健但不過熱，美股短線有壓然無

本行先前提及「美股通常會在升息初期承壓，但若
非如同2022年般的連續性升息，股市的趨勢不至於
受到影響」，此定調仍延續。雖然市場不時會處於
「壞消息才是好消息」的情緒當中，看到表現不錯
的經濟數據反而造成市場的震盪修正，但長期來說，
穩健的經濟基本面才是股市行情延續的關鍵，而目
前的景氣和企業獲利支持這樣的趨勢，只是高利率
環境仍將使市場資金更加嚴格看待資產的成長性，
具有強健資產負債表使其較不易受到融資成本上升

影響、具防禦性或差異化成長動能的企業，將能維
持相對韌性。
重點摘要
• 雖然市場不時會處於「壞消
息才是好消息」的情緒當中，
看到表現不錯的經濟數據反
而造成市場的震盪修正，但
長期來說，穩健的經濟基本
面才是股市行情延續的關鍵，
而目前的景氣和企業獲利支
持這樣的趨勢，只是高利率
環境仍將使市場資金更加嚴
格看待資產的成長性
4
正向的經濟環境有利股市行情延續

（1985-2025年）

《拉美股市-巴西魯拉意外逆轉勝選，市場重新定價
財政前景，股市評等下調為中立》
巴西總統大選出現戲劇性逆轉，現任左翼總統魯拉
在10月初第一輪投票落後的情況下，於第二輪決選
中逆轉，擊敗右翼對手小波索納洛，這是1989年恢

債匯市均出現劇烈下跌。
並不能說魯拉當選對巴西的經濟不利，他的前次當
選也並未引起股市震盪。巴西股市在估值的吸引力、
礦產的發展，都仍具有長線優勢。然目前資本市場
確實較青睞較為親美和親商的波索納洛，巴西股市8
月中以來的漲幅不小，很大程度是反映波索納洛取
得優勢，因此市場需要修正先前的定價，外資撤出。
另外，魯拉第一輪投票後為挽救選情，承諾大量財
政政策，如擴大所得稅扣除額、提高家庭補助金等，

在全球主要國家多面臨財政風險之際，市場對財政
前景的檢視力道將加大，利率上升壓力將提高。據
此，將拉美股市和巴西股市的評等由正向下調為中
立。

• 巴西魯拉意外逆轉勝選，市

上升壓力將提高。據此，將
拉美股市和巴西股市的評等
由正向下調為中立。

性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和

2026年 11月 12日 全5頁

ハイパースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に
与える影響 
日本の実質GDP成長率を0.19％pt押し上げ、下振れ時は半分以下に

経済調査部 エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁 
  チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司

[要約] 
◼ ハイパースケーラー（本稿ではマイクロソフトやアマゾンなど米企業 5社）の設備投
資は一段と加速し、半導体などの AI 関連需要を通じて世界経済を押し上げてきた。
FRBによると、AI関連需要は 2022年 1-3月期から 2026年 1-3月期までの米国の実質
GDPの累積的な伸びを 1.49％pt押し上げた。日本について同様に試算すると、同時期
の押し上げ幅は 0.85％ptにとどまる。国内投資の規模が米国に見劣りするうえ、純輸
出も増えておらず、AI関連需要を十分に取り込めていない。 
◼ 2027年の米国内におけるハイパースケーラーの設備投資が各国・地域の実質 GDP成長
率に与える影響を試算すると、平均的な民間アナリスト予想（前年比＋49.0％）に基
づく場合、台湾が 1.47％ptと最も大きく、米国が 1.05％pt、メキシコが 0.95％ptと
続き、日本も 0.19％ptとなる。ただし、設備投資の伸びが民間アナリスト予想の最小
値（同＋19.9％）にとどまれば、押し上げ効果は全ての国・地域で半分以下となる。
収益性や財務面への懸念、米中間選挙後の規制強化の可能性などから投資の先行きに
は不透明感があり、株価下落など金融市場を通じた景気の下振れにも注意が必要だ。

ハイパースケーラーの設備投資が急拡大、米中間選挙後の先行きには不透明感 
世界の半導体販売額は 2024年に前年比＋20％、2025年に同＋26％と高い伸びを続けてきた
が、2026年は増加ペースが一段と加速している。2026年 1～9月の累計販売額は前年同期比＋
123％となり、既に 2025年通年額を上回った。仮にこのペースで販売額が拡大すると、2026年
通年の販売額は1.8兆ドルに達する（図表 1左）。  
こうした急拡大の背景にあるのが、米国のハイパースケーラー1による設備投資の急増だ。
2024、25年には四半期平均で前期比＋14％超だったハイパースケーラーの設備投資額の伸び率

1 本稿ではハイパースケーラーとは、マイクロソフト、アマゾン、アルファベット、メタ、オラクルの 5社を
指す。

### Page 2

1

2 / 5

は、2026年 7-9月期までの実績で同＋20％超へと高まり、投資拡大のペースが加速している
（図表1右）。

図表1：世界半導体販売額（左）、ハイパースケーラーの設備投資額（右）

（注）右図は11月12日時点に入手したデータに基づく。オラクルの決算期は他社と1カ月のずれがある（オラ
クルの 2026年第 3四半期は 6-8月）。最大値（最小値）は各社設備投資額の民間アナリスト予想の最大値（最
小値）の合計。 
（出所）WSTS、Bloombergより大和総研作成

こうした急ピッチの投資拡大が今後も続くかは不透明だ。11月 12日時点の民間アナリスト
予想によると、ハイパースケーラーの設備投資額は 2027年にかけて四半期平均で前期比＋8％
弱の伸びが見込まれている。2027年通年では前年比＋49.0％が平均的な見通しだが、予想には
大きな幅があり、最大値では同＋74.7％、最小値では同＋19.9％となる（図表 1右）。 
AI関連サービスの世界的な利用拡大に伴い、計算能力の増強に向けた大型投資が今後も続く
可能性は高い。一方で、一部のAI関連企業では収益性や財務面への懸念が広がっているほか、
米中間選挙でAIデータセンター建設の一時停止や規制強化などを訴えた民主党急進左派候補者
が当選するなど、AI投資を取り巻く環境には変化の兆しも見られる。こうした中、AI関連銘柄
の株価はこのところ軟調に推移している。ハイパースケーラーが設備投資の拡大ペースを落と
せば、半導体や情報通信機器などの需要を下押しし、関連産業の生産減などを通じて実体経済
にも波及するだろう。

AI関連投資の成長押し上げ効果、足元では日本が米国を下回る 
ハイパースケーラーを中心とするAI関連投資の急拡大は、米国景気を下支えしてきた。米連
邦準備制度理事会（FRB）は、情報通信機器やソフトウェアなどへの投資が、2022年 1-3月期
から 2026年 1-3月期までの米国の実質 GDPの累積的な伸びを 1.49％pt押し上げたと試算して

いる2（図表 2 左）。 情報通信機器への投資の増加は、ハイパースケーラーの設備投資が急拡大
し始めた時期と一致しており、データセンター向けサーバーなどへの投資が増加したとみられ
る。2025年末以降は、米国内の供給だけでは需要を賄いきれず、情報通信機器などの輸入が急
増している。それでも、輸入による実質 GDPの押し下げ分を考慮しても、AI関連投資などによ
る成長率の押し上げ効果は大きい。 
FRBの手法を参考に、日本でも同様の試算を行った結果が図表 2右だ。日本はソフトウェア
投資を中心に 2022年から 2024年にかけて堅調な伸びが続き、2024年末時点では AI関連投資
などによる実質 GDP成長率の累積寄与度は米国を上回っていた。しかし、2025年以降は米国を
下回り、ソフトウェア投資の一服などもあって、2026年1-3月期時点の累積寄与度は0.85％pt
と米国の6割弱にとどまる。加えて、情報通信機器等の純輸出の寄与度は2025年半ば以降、横
ばい圏で推移している。AI関連需要が世界的に急拡大する中でも、日本では輸出の増加が輸入
の増加を上回るには至っていない。中国や韓国、台湾の競争力が高まる中、日本は電気機械の
最終財で比較優位が低下しており3、AI関連需要の拡大を国内生産や輸出に十分につなげられ
ていないとみられる。

図表2：実質 GDP成長率に対する AI関連需要の累積寄与度（左：米国、右：日本）

（注）左図はFRBによる試算結果。右図は四半期別GDP速報の推計手法を参考にしつつ、大和総研にて寄与度を
試算した結果。FRB の試算と同様に、右図の情報通信機器の純輸出からは設備投資以外の最終需要に配分され
る割合を控除するための処理を施している。日本の電力設備やデータセンターへの投資はデータの制約があ
り、取り上げていない。

4 / 5

ハイパースケーラーの投資拡大は日本の成長率を0.19％pt押し上げも、下振れ時は半分以下に 
拡大するハイパースケーラーの設備投資は、今後、日本を含む世界経済にどのような影響を
与えるだろうか。前掲図表 1右で示した民間アナリスト予想をもとに、2027年におけるハイパ
ースケーラーの米国内での設備投資が主要国・地域の実質 GDP成長率をどの程度押し上げるか
を試算した結果が図表 3である。 
ハイパースケーラーによる投資拡大の恩恵を最も受けるのは台湾である。民間アナリスト予
想の平均（前年比＋49.0％）が実現した場合の実質GDP成長率の押し上げ幅は1.47％ptと、一
次波及効果だけで米国の押し上げ幅1.05％ptを上回る。10月30日に公表された2026年7-9月
期における台湾の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋13.5％と非常に高い伸びとなったように、台
湾は急拡大するハイパースケーラーの設備投資需要を取り込んでおり、これが足元の高成長を
支える一因となっている。 
メキシコへの波及効果も大きい。AI関連需要の拡大に伴ってメキシコのコンピューターサー
バー輸出は急増4しており、米国の隣国という地理的要因もあって、米国の堅調な設備投資の恩
恵を受けている。 
日本に目を向けると、実質GDPの押し上げ幅は0.19％ptと、ハイパースケーラーの投資拡大
の恩恵を一定程度受けると試算される。しかし、台湾や米国、メキシコ、韓国と比べると波及
効果は半分以下にとどまる。背景には、前掲図表2右で示したように、日本はAI関連需要を経
済成長に取り込みにくい産業構造にあることが挙げられる。また、日本の一次波及効果は中国
やドイツを上回るものの、雇用者報酬や営業余剰に対する個人消費や設備投資の弾性値が他
国・地域よりも低く、二次波及効果まで含めると中国やドイツを下回る。賃金や企業収益の増
加が個人消費や設備投資の拡大につながりにくいことも、波及効果を弱める一因だ。 
一方、ハイパースケーラーの設備投資額が伸び悩み、民間アナリスト予想の最小値（前年比
＋19.9％）程度にとどまった場合、実質 GDP 成長率の押し上げ効果は大幅に縮小し、全ての
国・地域で半分以下にまで落ち込む。 
この場合、ハイパースケーラーの設備投資増の恩恵を受けてきた国・地域ほど実質 GDP成長
率の下振れ幅も大きくなる。下振れ幅は台湾では0.75％pt程度、米国では0.7％pt程度、メキ
シコでは0.5％pt程度と試算され、こうした国・地域への影響には注意が必要だ。 
今回の試算には、AI関連需要の持続性への懸念から株価が下落するなど、金融市場を通じた
影響は含まれていない。前述のようにAI関連銘柄の株価はこのところ軟調に推移しているが、
ハイパースケーラーの設備投資の増加ペースが大幅に減速すれば金融市場に不安が広がり、株
価の調整が一段と進む可能性がある。株価下落は逆資産効果を通じて各国・地域の個人消費を
抑制するため、実質 GDP成長率の下押し幅は図表3の試算よりも大きくなる可能性がある。

4 詳細は“Picture This: Mexico’s Server Boom Reshapes AI Data Center Supply Chains”(S&P Global 
Market Intelligence, August 12, 2026)を参照。

### Page 5

1

5 / 5

図表3：ハイパースケーラーの設備投資による実質 GDP成長率押し上げ効果の試算（2027年）

（注1）「実質GDP押し上げ幅」（「ハイパースケーラーの設備投資下振れ時」）は、2027年のハイパースケーラー
の設備投資額が、前掲図表 1 右で示した民間アナリスト予想平均（最小値）である前年比＋49.0％（同＋
19.9％）となった場合の効果。「一次波及効果」は米国内でのハイパースケーラーの設備投資増加が各国・地
域の生産増につながり、実質GDP成長率を押し上げる効果。「二次波及効果」はハイパースケーラーの設備投資
増加による各国・地域の生産増が、雇用者報酬と営業余剰の増加につながり、それらが個人消費と設備投資を
押し上げる効果。「直接効果」はハイパースケーラーの設備投資増加が米国の総固定資本形成に計上され、米
国の実質 GDP成長率を押し上げる効果。本試算は価格や為替レート、投入構造が不変であるとの仮定を置いて
おり、需要増加による価格変動や供給制約は織り込まれていない。 
（注 2）2027年における米国の総固定資本形成額（OECD予測値）とハイパースケーラーの設備投資額の比とハ
イパースケーラーの売上高に占める米国比率を踏まえ、民間アナリスト予想平均（最小値）の伸び率が実現し
た場合、米国の総固定資本形成額は 3.1％程度（1.2％程度）増加し、業種別には、電子・電気機器への投資が
20％（10％）、一般機械への投資が4.5％（2.5％）、建設投資が2％（0.75％）、その他の財・サービスへの投資
が 1％（0％）増加すると仮定。直接効果は輸入増加による実質 GDP押し下げ効果を控除している。 
（注 3）試算に用いた国際産業連関表は ADBの 2024年時点版と OECDの 2022年時点版であるため、足元の AI関
連需要の急拡大を完全には織り込めていない可能性がある点には留意が必要。また、ハイパースケーラーが米
国外で行う設備投資も各国・地域の実質 GDP成長率を押し上げると想定されるが、ハイパースケーラーの米国
外での設備投資額・売上高は国別では公表されていないため今回の試算には含んでいない。

Document 498: フィリピン経済はスタグフレーションの様相、マルコス政権への逆風が強まる ～ペソ安と物価高、景気低迷の悪循環が続くなか、2028_02fa0204

Asia Trends

2026 年11 月 10 日

1/5 
フィリピン経済はスタグフレーションの様相、マルコス政権への逆風が強まる 
～ペソ安と物価高、景気低迷の悪循環が続くなか、2028 年大統領選への影響にも注目～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 足元のフィリピン経済を巡っては、イラン情勢の悪化に伴う原油高やペソ安を背景とするイ
ンフレ加速と、景気低迷が同時進行するスタグフレーションに直面している。中銀は断続的
な利上げを実施しているものの、金融政策の遅れや対外収支の悪化への懸念からペソ安圧力
は収まらず、10 月のインフレ率は前年比＋7.8％に加速した。 
○ ７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率も前期比年率＋0.28％に鈍化しており、物価高や金融引き締めに
よる個人消費の減速、公共事業を巡る汚職疑惑に伴う政府支出の停滞などが景気の重しとな
っている。2026 年も政府成長率目標（＋3.5～4.5％）の達成は極めて困難とみられ、４年連
続の未達となるなど、マルコス政権にとっては求心力の低下が懸念される。 
○ 2028 年の次期大統領選挙に向けては、物価対策や雇用改善、汚職対策を通じた政治への信頼
回復が重要な争点となろう。先行きは、原油価格の安定や公共投資の正常化が景気回復のカ
ギを握る一方、政治的対立の長期化や財政悪化への懸念がリスクとなる。ペソ相場の持続的
な安定には、金融引き締めだけでなく、対外収支の改善や国内経済の成長力回復を通じた金
融市場からの信認回復が不可欠と考えられる。

【断続的な利上げ実施も、金融市場は「遅れ」や対外収支の悪化を警戒してペソ安が収まらず】 
フィリピン金融市場では、10 月末に通貨ペソの対ドル相場が最安値を更新するなど、資金流出に
直面している（図１）。背景には、中銀による金融政策の遅れ（behind the curve）が警戒されてい
ることがある。イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油高は世界的なエネルギーインフレを招くなか、同国
は原油需要の９割以上を輸入に依存するなど、その影響を受けやすい構造にある。イラン情勢の悪
化による供給懸念の高まりを受けて、マルコス政権はエネルギー非常事態を宣言し、エネルギー調
達の多様化に取り組む方針を示した。中銀は直後に緊急会合を参集したため、金融市場では利上げ
観測が強まったものの、金利を据え置く『肩透かし』の対応をみせた。さらに、中銀はペソ安を容

### Page 2

2/5 
認しているとも受け取れる見解を示した
ため、ペソ安が一段と進むことにつなが
った。 
その後、中銀は４月の定例会合で約２
年半ぶりの利上げに舵を切り、６月、８
月の定例会合でも追加利上げに動くとと
もに、10 月の定例会合では大幅利上げに
より金融引き締めを強めた（ 注１）。この
ように、中銀による断続的な利上げ実施
にもかかわらず、その後もペソ安の動き
は収まらなかった。こうしたなか、直近
10 月のインフレ率は前年比＋7.8％に加
速し、中銀の目標レンジ（２～４％）を
大きく上回り、2023 年２月以来の高い伸
びとなった（図２）。食料品とエネルギー
を 除 い た コ ア イ ン フ レ 率 も 前 年 比 ＋
5.4％に加速して目標レンジを上回り、
2023 年９月以来の高い伸びとなるなど、
幅広くインフレ圧力が強まる動きがみら
れる。前述のように金融市場におけるペ
ソ安が収まらず、輸入物価に押し上げ圧
力がかかる動きも足元のインフレを助長
していると考えられる。

注１ 10 月22 日付レポート「フィリピン中銀、原油高とペソ安の「二重苦」に直面し、大幅利上げに舵」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666821.html）

### Page 3

3/5 
金融市場においてペソ安が収まらない背景には、対外収支の脆弱性が高まっていることもある。
同国はエネルギー資源のみならず、主食のコメをはじめとする食料品も輸入に依存しており、国際
商品市況の高止まりが続くなかで輸入額
が押し上げられ、貿易赤字が拡大してい
る。資金流出の動きも重なり、外貨準備
高は減少している。とはいえ、足元の外
貨準備高の水準はＩＭＦ（国際通貨基金）
が国際金融市場の動揺への耐性の有無を
示すＡＲＡ（適正水準評価）に照らして
「適正水準（100～150％）」を上回ると試
算される（図３）。このため、同国を発端
にした危機的状況に至る事態は想定され
ないものの、対外環境の悪化が長期化す
れば、脆弱さへの懸念が高まるリスクには注意が必要である。

【７-９月GDPは一段と鈍化、2026 年も成長率目標のハードルは高く、政権の死に体化は必至か】 
こうしたなか、７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前期比年率＋0.28％とプラス成長で推移している
ものの、前期（同＋2.21％）から拡大ペースは鈍化してコロナ禍の影響が色濃く現れた 2020 年４-
６月以来の低い伸びとなった（図４）。中
期的な基調を示す前年同期比ベースの
成長率も＋2.1％と前期（同＋2.4％）か
ら伸びが鈍化しており、コロナ禍以来の
低成長となるなど足元の景気が頭打ち
の動きを強めている様子がうかがえる。
世界的なＡＩ（人工知能）・半導体関連需
要の旺盛さを追い風に、アジアでは輸出
が押し上げられる動きがみられるもの
の、同国にはそうした勢いに乗れない展
開が続いている。また、インフレが長期
化しており、物価抑制を目的とする中銀による断続的な利上げ実施も重なる形で実質購買力に下押
し圧力がかかり、経済成長のけん引役となってきた個人消費の勢いに陰りがでている。ＧＤＰの１
割に相当する海外からの移民送金も頭打ちとなっていることも、個人消費の足かせとなっている。
同国政界では公共事業を巡る汚職疑惑がマルコス大統領の身内や側近に及ぶなか、事業の審査や執
行の見直しが進んでおり、政府消費の重しとなる展開が続いている。一方で、低迷が続いた企業部
門による設備投資に持ち直しの動きがみられるとともに、一部の公共投資で進捗の動きがみられる
ことも重なり、固定資本投資は２四半期ぶりの拡大に転じた。しかし、内外需が幅広く勢いを欠く

### Page 4

4/5 
なか、足元の景気低迷を打開するには至っていない。 
分野ごとの生産動向を巡っては、個人消費をはじめとする内需が力強さを欠いていることを反映
して、サービス業の生産は鈍化している。さらに、台風の相次ぐ襲来をはじめとする異常気象の頻
発が影響する形で農林漁業の生産は大きく下振れしており、景気の足を引っ張っている。一方で、
縫製業など一部の製造業の生産に持ち直しの動きがみられるものの、原油をはじめとする原材料価
格の高止まりや供給懸念が足かせとなる形で機械関連や化学関連など幅広い分野で生産は低迷し
ている。このように、足元の同国景気を巡っては、需要サイドのみならず、供給サイドもともに低
迷しているうえ、供給面を主因とする物価高に直面しており、スタグフレーションに陥っていると
捉えられる。 
同国政府は、2026 年の経済成長率目標を＋3.5～4.5％としているが、９月までの累計ベースの成
長率は＋2.5％と目標下限を大きく下回る伸びにとどまる。バリサカン経済企画開発相は、ＧＤＰ
統計の発表に際して、成長率目標の実現には 10-12 月の成長率が前年比＋6.3％程度となる必要が
あるとして、極めて難しいとの認識を示した。2022 年にマルコス政権が発足して以降、2025 年ま
で３年連続で成長率目標をクリアできない展開が続いてきたが、2026 年もそのハードルは高いと
見込まれる。その意味では、マルコス政権は任期満了まで約１年半を残しているものの、残りの任
期を巡っては「死に体（レームダック）」化が進む可能性は高まっていると判断できる。

【2028 年の次期大統領選挙へは、物価対策などの経済政策、政治の信頼回復の行方がカギに】 
こうした経済情勢の悪化は、2028 年５月に実施が予定される次期大統領選挙を巡る政治環境に
も影響を及ぼすと考えられる。現職のマルコス大統領は憲法上、再選が認められていない。そのた
め、次期大統領選挙に向けては、後継候補を巡る動きと、現政権に批判的な勢力の動向に焦点が当
たっている。すでに、2022 年の大統領選でマルコス氏と協力関係にあったサラ・ドゥテルテ＝カル
ピオ副大統領が次期大統領選への出馬を表明しており、両者の政治的対立が鮮明になっている。た
だし、サラ氏を巡っては、資産申告などに関する疑惑を巡る弾劾裁判が進められており（注２）、足
元では弁護側による証人尋問が行われている。サラ氏自身は一連の疑惑を否定しているものの、弾
劾裁判の結果次第では公職への就任資格に影響が及ぶ可能性がある。そのため、現時点においては、
次期大統領選の候補者構成を見通すことは難しい状況にある。 
一方で、有権者の関心は、マルコス家とドゥテルテ家の政治的な対立に加え、生活に直結する経
済問題にも向かっている。世論調査においては、有権者が関心を抱く課題の上位にインフレ対策を
はじめとする政府の経済対策のほか、汚職対策が挙がっている。こうした結果は、物価高による生
活費負担の増大のみならず、政治に対する信頼回復が重要な関心事となっていることを示唆してい
る。今後も景気低迷やペソ安を通じた物価上昇が長期化すれば、マルコス政権の経済運営に対する
評価や後継候補の政策方針を巡る議論に影響する可能性が考えられる。他方、物価上昇の沈静化や

注２ ７月７日付レポート「フィリピン･サラ副大統領への弾劾裁判開始」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/623855.html）

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
公共事業の正常化、雇用環境の改善などが進めば、経済情勢を巡る有権者の評価も変化し得る。こ
うしたことから、2028 年の大統領選への影響を判断するうえでは、今後１年以上にわたる家計の実
質所得や雇用環境の変化を見極める必要があろう。

【先行きは景気回復と金融市場からの信認回復が不可欠に、政治情勢の動きには引き続き注意】 
先行きのフィリピン経済を巡っては、国際原油価格の動向と公共投資の回復が景気を左右する重
要な要因となることが予想される。仮にイラン情勢が好転するとともに、原油価格が落ち着きを取
り戻せば、輸入額の減少を通じた対外収支の改善やインフレ圧力の後退が期待される。そうした動
きは、経済成長のけん引役となってきた家計消費の回復のほか、ペソ相場の安定を促す可能性があ
る。また、公共事業の透明性向上への取り組みに加え、適切な執行が再開されることは、建設投資
の回復のみならず、投資家の信認を取り戻すうえでも重要になる。一方で、原油価格の高止まりや
政治的対立の長期化などが足かせとなる形で景気低迷が続けば、マルコス政権にとっては財政拡大
などを通じて景気を下支えしようとする誘因が強まる。しかし、そうした対応は財政悪化懸念を招
くとともに、ペソ安を通じたインフレ圧力を一段と強めるリスクもある。次期大統領選が近づくに
つれて、経済政策を巡る議論が活発化すると見込まれるが、政府には財政規律と経済成長の両立を
図りつつ、金融市場からの信認を回復できるかが問われる。ペソ相場の持続的な安定には、金融引
き締めのみに依存するのではなく、対外収支の改善と国内経済の成長力回復を通じて、通貨安をも
たらす構造的な要因を和らげていくことも不可欠であろう。 
以 上

動態更新 化學品 ‧ 台灣

三福化工 
(4755.TW/4755 TT)

半導體材料漸成營運重心 
重要訊息 
三福化公告 3Q26 財報。 
評論及分析 
3Q26 利潤率低於預期，但業外收益挹注獲利。三福化 3Q26 營收 13.3 億
元，季增 8%、年增 13%，分別優於市場共識與凱基預估 2%與 6%，主因
先進封裝需求強勁，光阻剝離液 (InFO+CoWoS) 營收季增 40%、年增
210%。但受到 7-8 月歲修影響，加上夏季電價，毛利率季減 1.1 個百分點
至 23.0%，低於市場共識的 24.4%與凱基預估的 25.8%。且子公司新藥開
發推升單季費用，導致營益率季減 1.8、年增 3.4 個百分點至 10.1%。受惠
於業外貢獻 (處分土地與匯兌利益)，稅後淨利季增 99%、年增 176%至
2.65 億元，折合每股盈餘 2.63 元。 
來自半導體的訂單強勁。CoWoS Stripper 3Q26 營收季增 53%、年增
288%，除了 CoWoS 需求強勁外，WMCM 亦採用同一料號，並於 8 月開始
出貨；FOPLP 與 CoPoS 仍在認證，預期 2Q27 後開始貢獻。在 CoWoS 與
WMCM 月產持續提升下，我們預估 2027 年三福化 Stripper 營收將年增
74%。另一方面，公司展望 2027 年 TMAH 工程收入將達 6-7 億元 (對比今
年僅預估達 2.5 億元)，我們認為有望帶動半導體營收比重將從 2026 年的
35%提升至 2027 年的 44%，且隨著產品組合優化將帶動毛利率上揚。 
2027 年本業獲利成長逾 5 成。考量先進封裝持續擴產、工程收入放大，以
及 PHBA 出貨量提升 (公司展望 2027 年達 4,000-4,500 噸)，我們預估三福
化 2027 年營收將年增 22%至 61.8 億元。隨著產品組合改善與產能利用率
提高，我們預估 2027 年毛利率將年增 4.1 個百分點至 27.9%。我們預估 
2026-27 年每股盈餘分別為 6.66 元與 8.82 元。若排除 3Q26 處分土地的一
次性收益 (約 1.1-1.2 元)，2027 年本業獲利將年增逾 5 成。 
投資建議 
考量先進封裝擴產動能延續，加上 2027 年工程收入放大與多項半導體新品
陸續放量，我們維持「增加持股」評等，並調升目標價至 220.5 元，基於
2027 年 EPS 預估 25 倍換算得出。 
投資風險 
先進封裝需求下降；工程進度遞延。 
增加持股‧維持 
  收盤價 November 11 (NT$)  121.5

焦點內容  
1. 3Q26 營收優於預期，惟歲修影響毛
利率表現。 
2. 先進封裝擴產與工程收入放大，帶
動半導體營收比重持續提升。 
3. 上修 2027 年獲利預估 3%，本業獲
利將年增逾五成。

台灣 三福化工

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

圖 5: 公司概況  圖 6: 精密化學品為主要營收來源 
三福化工於 2003 年自三福氣體公司分割後獨立的公司，主要從
事生產精密化學品、基礎化學品與新興化學品，廣泛應用於半導
體、面板、太陽能等產業。2025 年營收比重為精密化學品
51%、基礎化學品 28%、新興化學品 20%。

Document 586: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_4576 大銀微系統_莊政翰_20261111

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_Takeaway_4576 大銀微系統_莊政翰_20261111.pdf
Report source: line報告備份

### Page 1

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

線上法說會重點摘要

大銀微系統 
(4576 TT, NT$194.5, 未評等)

焦點內容 
1. 3Q26 營收季增 19%、年增 77%至 12.18 億元，其中定位平台營收年
增 101%，佔整體營收 58%。受惠高毛利平台出貨佔比提高及規模經
濟，毛利率季增 5.3、年增 8.7 個百分點至 42.4%。 
2. CPO相關訂單已排至 2027 年年中。矽光子檢測平台 2026 年 4 月開始
供貨，8 月取得量產續單，目前已貢獻營收。公司預期 2027 年高階定
位平台需求延續。 
3. 高階定位平台具較高附加價值，有助推升毛利率，第二階段擴產預
計自 2027 年 4 月貢獻營收。元件業務則透過產線自動化提高產
出，帶動營收成長，中長期目標為將元件營收佔比由約 40% 提升
至 45-50%。 
結論 
CPO 訂單與新產能開出可望延續 2027 年高階定位平台的出貨動能，提升
整體毛利率。元件業務透過產線自動化增加產出，將增添營收成長動能。

營運展望 
 依目前接單及出貨組合，公司預期 4Q26 毛利率約 40%。2027 年
隨新產能開出及新應用導入，毛利率目標亦為 40%，實際表現視
出貨組合而定。 
 2027 年資本支出初估 1.2-1.5 億元，主要用於高階定位平台擴產、
元件產線自動化、精密加工設備及生產系統更新。 
 微米級及奈米級定位平台第一階段擴產已完成。第二階段預計於 
2027 年 2 月底至 3 月中完成，約一個月後開始產出，預計 4 月反
映在營收。 
 第三階段擴產已開始規劃，動工時程將視客戶新專案及訂單意向而
定。 
 高階定位平台訂單能見度約 5-6 個月，交期約 3 個月。元件訂單能
見度約 3 個月，交期約 2-2.5 個月。 
 工程師不足限制高階定位平台放量。公司持續招募工程師，並規劃
隨元件產線自動化，將調派部分人力支援高階平台。 
 公司加強與物料供應商合作，並增加磁鐵等備料。管理層表示，物
料供應狀況正逐步改善。 
 高階定位平台具較高附加價值，有助支撐整體毛利率。元件業務則
將持續推動產線半自動化及自動化，以提高產出、帶動營收成長。
公司中長期目標為將元件營收佔比由約 40%提升至 45-50%。

### Page 2

台灣 大銀微系統

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

 公司將以 1-Nano Controller、Tip-Tilt 高精度五軸馬達及 MD-ZT 多
維度定位方案，因應晶圓及大面積面板翹曲，目標提高平台 ASP 與
高階應用營收佔比。 
 精密力量控制技術正依計畫開發，規劃由非接觸式量測與檢測延伸
至精密鑽孔、塔式分選及混合鍵合（Hybrid Bonding ）等接觸式製
程。 
 2027 年 1-Nano Controller 維持搭配高階定位平台銷售。研發重點
包括控制演算法、增益頻寬、位置／速度／電流三環控制，以及參
數調校與驗證。 
 元件端將持續擴充驅動器與控制器的應用程式庫，以降低客戶導入
難度，帶動驅控元件及馬達銷售。 
 CPO 相關訂單已排至 2027 年年中。矽光子檢測平台 2026 年 4 月
開始供貨，8 月取得量產續單，目前已貢獻營收。現階段供應 CPO
製程 Insertion 3，主要用於光學引擎及系統級測試。 
財報及營運更新 
 3Q26 營收季增 19%、年增 77%至 12.18 億元，主要由定位平台
帶動。定位平台營收季增 32%、年增 101%至 7.06 億元，佔整體
營收 58%。元件營收季增 3%、年增 49%至 4.94 億元，佔比
41%。 
 3Q26 毛利率季增 5.3、年增 8.7 個百分點至 42.4%，主因高毛利
定位平台出貨佔比提高及規模經濟效益。 
 3Q26 營業費用率降至 18.1%，帶動營業利益率季增 6.3、年增 
13.9 個百分點至 24.3%。 
 3Q26 EPS 季增 123%、年增 257%至 2.14 元。業外由淨支出轉為
淨收益，主因匯兌轉盈。 
 3Q26 美洲、台灣、其他亞洲地區營收分別季增 21%、51%及 
15%，年增 127%、101%及 89%。歐洲營收季增 5%、年減
12%。 
 美國期中選舉後，公司向美國、台灣及其他亞洲客戶確認，截至今
日，現有及新增專案、需求預測、出貨台數及交期均未見變動。 
 半導體及 CPO 相關平台毛利率最高，精密量測與檢測次之，PCB、
電子製造及自動化用平台較低。管理層表示，定位精度越高、整定
時間越短，平台毛利率越高。 
 高階定位平台兼顧移動速度與定位穩定度，有助前段晶圓檢測設備
在高速移動時維持成像品質。 
 1-Nano Controller 的伺服穩定度達±1nm，搭配 50-100 倍顯微鏡
可維持取像清晰，並支援秒級尋光，以提高光耦合效率。 
 公司整合自有控制器、驅動器、馬達及位置量測系統，搭配上銀
(2049 TT, NT$314.0 ，增加持股) 的線性滑軌與滾珠螺桿，供應客
製化定位平台，並可延伸至晶圓搬送與設備前端模組（EFEM）。

台灣 大銀微系統

 集團估計關鍵元件自給率逾 85%，其中控制器為 75%，驅動器、
線性馬達及 AC 馬達均為 100%。垂直整合有助縮短開發時程、降
低介面整合風險及擴大單機供應範圍。 
 高階平台所需的驅動器、控制器及直驅馬達均備有庫存。管理層表
示，其中 98%為標準品，有助加快打樣及交付。 
 元件端提供 HIMC3 運動控制器、E2 驅動器及直驅馬達。驅動器支
援 EtherCAT、PROFINET、EtherNet/IP 及 MECHATROLINK，客戶可
沿用既有上位控制器，搭配大銀微系統元件用於 PCB 設備、電子製
造及智慧自動化。

市場展望投資策略
 短期風險資產重新訂價，關注法債：近期股市回檔推升
債券價格，美債殖利率大幅回落，把握5%殖利率水準分
批布局中長天期美國公債或投資等級債ETF。另建議關
注法債，當前與德債的利差具收斂空間，且相對估值便
宜，回測當前殖利率水準作為長期買點勝率100%。(詳
見Page 12、13)
 逢回加碼科技股、儲能工業：AI投資疑慮再起，科技股
財報考驗市場，引發科技股震盪。AI因推論以及企業應
用推升電力，傳統工業巨頭深耕新品滿足AI高壓電力需
求，關注廣泛的AI受惠儲能工業。(詳見Page 16、17)
 GPU租賃價格續高，韓國及台灣股市受惠：儘管AI需求
受到質疑，但數據顯示，在會計6年折舊準則之下，
GPU資產市場價值卻遠高於公允價值，反映AI真實需求
帶來的AI晶片維持高溢價，且海力士記憶體財報優於預
期，關注受AI晶片受惠的台韓國家股市相關類股。(詳見
Page 19)
 匯市展望：近期外匯市場相對股債平靜，歐元受經濟數

據及法國政治波動影響呈現結構性疲軟，美元因通膨持
續攀升及未來升息步伐明確，在FOMC會議後將持續重
新訂價，預估美元指數強勢走揚，本周美元指數區間看
在99.50~102.5。
 債市展望：近期股市AI敘事受到挑戰，資金迅速轉向防
禦類資產，債券殖利率短期在各國升息政策下迅速回落，
預期將會持續影響債市，本周10年美債殖利率區間看在
4.90%~5.05%。
 股市展望: CSP財報表現分歧，拖累整體大盤表現，且期
中選舉仍有不確定風險，指數可能出現震盪整理，策略
上應採取選股不選市，著重基本面，本周標普500指數
區間看在7,550~7,700。

頭條新聞
1. 美國聯準會以10比2的投票結果維持利率不變，重申遏
制通膨的決心，但未對12月的利率行動做出暗示。主席
Warsh表示，當前金融情勢已有所緊縮，明年起將減少
利率決策次數，相關工作小組亦預計於明年第一季初提
出資產負債表建議供委員會研議。 (詳見Page 5)
2. 本周美國期中選舉，美國國內汽油平均零售價漲破拜登
任內平均水準，共和黨在期中選舉面臨嚴峻的民意壓力。
市場普遍認為民主黨有機會奪回眾議院，但翻轉參議院
的難度較高，然而風險趨緩有利市場。(詳見Page 6)
3. 巴西現任左派總統Lula在二輪決選中逆轉勝出連任，押
注右翼將整頓財政的市場措手不及，因此進一步擔憂新
政府恐難以抑制赤字與債務擴大，觸發巴西股匯市場雙
雙重挫，咖啡、黃豆、糖等大宗商品期貨同步走跌。
產業訊息
1. 甲骨文對長期自由現金流的展望優於預期，推升股價大
漲逾10%。科技巨頭財報表現分歧，微軟上季Azure雲端
業務收入超出預期強勁增長48%，帶動盤後股價大漲，

反觀Alphabet季度營收與獲利令人失望，盤後股價重挫。
2. 大型科技企業與金融機構的融資規模合計再增加1,980億
美元。Meta正尋求950億美元的股權融資以支應AI支出，
而博通與華爾街達成630億美元融資協議，用於採購供
Anthropic使用的晶片，SpaceX則與阿波羅全球管理簽
署價值約400億美元的輝達AI晶片融資租賃協議。
3. SK海力士公布強勁財報，本季度營收達104兆韓元(約
780億美元)，年增328%，優於市場預期的98兆韓元，營
業利益達88兆韓元，高於預期的76兆韓元。公司計畫向
投資人返還高達750億美元資本，並已與14家主要客戶簽
署長期供貨協議，鎖定2027年與2028年超過五成的產能，
預期記憶體需求短缺將一路延續至2030年。

### Page 5

FOMC維持利率不變，升息預期減弱
5
本次FOMC預估隱含利率較前次明顯下降 圖表解讀
➢ Fed以10票贊成、2票反對通過維持基準利率於3.75%
～4.00%區間不變，符合市場預期。庫克與哈馬克主
張再升息1碼。經濟評估上調為「強勁擴張」，主席
Warsh強調抗通膨仍為首要任務。
➢ Warsh依舊不使用前瞻指引，故12月動向未明，會中
指出AI投資無法完全免疫於利率變動，但強調經濟強
勁非單靠科技巨頭。通膨方面，物價漲幅仍偏高，強
調通膨及時回歸2%目標的立場，另自2027年起調整會
議安排，減少1次例會，2028年減少2次例會。
➢ 利率會議後，殖利率先短升再迅速走跌，2年期國債殖
利率降幅較大，市場預期年底升息1碼機率為82.2%。
此外，2027年至少升息二碼的機率較前次會後由88%
下降至56.4%。

6

民主黨勝選正當性所發動的大規模調查。

索最新民調顯示，總統川普支持率由上周的31%回升1
個百分點至32%，仍接近該調查紀錄低點。

票結果符合市場預期，有利股市短期表現。

使全球債券殖利率大幅回落，由於短天期美日利差僅略
縮窄，因此幾乎沒有動力讓日圓走貶。然而，從基本面
角度，日本的財政風險限制日圓的支撐買盤。日本央行
維持現有政策路徑，日圓缺乏大幅升值的催化劑。

避險買盤，在市 場情緒稍 顯緩和 後出現獲利 了結 ，在
SNB防範幣值過強的立場下，瑞朗偏向溫和承壓震盪。

### Page 12

避險情緒延續，美債殖利率短期有望下行
12
9月FOMC會議以來高收益債相對跌深
圖表解讀
➢ 10月下旬後投資人將資金撤出高收益債，轉而大舉買
進投資級債。殖利率高檔震盪的環境下，市場資金更
靠攏資產的防禦力與信用評等資產，相對買盤支撐強
勁，建議逢低佈局優質企業債以鎖定多年罕見的較高
票息，對違約風險較高的高收益債則轉趨保守。
➢ 儘管10 月初， 美國10 年 期 公 債 殖 利 率 一 度 衝 高 至
5.31%，創下自2002年5月以來的新高紀錄，隨後殖利
率下跌主要受到全球股市，尤其是AI與科技股估值擔
憂的影響，引發了市場的避險買盤。此外，市場猜測
美國財政部可能在11月初的發債計畫中，釋出暫緩增
發長天期公債的訊號，雖有望在短期引導長端殖利率
下行，但財政部在面臨未來資金缺口的情況下，不太
可能削減長端供應。

本周美股財報預期正向
15
重點提醒

高安全級別的政府雲端環境，對沖市場對其高估值的疑

開始發放。此舉在調整企業文化，關注高通是否成功因
應消費電子不景氣與向AI資料中心轉型的陣痛期。

### Page 16

圖表解讀
➢ 傳統AI伺服器採用480VAC轉換鏈路經歷多級降壓整流，
整體電氣效率約為93%，導入800VDC架構後，減少
一道轉換層級，效率可拉升至95%。
➢ 看似僅有2個百分點的效率提升，但在超大規模資料中
心內將帶來驚人的營運成本節省，在100 MW IT負載
規模下，每年可省下約19,800 MWh電力，相當於年
省160萬美元電費。在1GW級別的AI園區中，年電費
節省可達約1,600萬美元。針對未來數GW規模的AI超
級工廠，年節省效益甚至突破1億美元，所以資料中心
配電架構正在積極推動向800V高壓直流HVDC演進。
➢ 對於電源供應商顯著受惠，其中一個最主要的影響是，
與傳統的機架內電源供應器解決方案相比，高壓直流
電源機櫃每瓦的價值含量高出約4-5倍，因為前者將更
多元件整合到一個堅固的系統中，而且電源供應商通
常會運送整個機櫃，而不僅僅是元件，有利獲利爆發。

股票
市場
美國 美國經濟仍維持溫和成長，AI資本支出持續，EPS成長領先。焦點轉向企業基本面與AI資本支出， 雲端、半導體及電力基礎建設仍是主要受惠族群，惟AI融資疑慮加劇，美股波動放大。
新亞 亞洲盈餘成長加速，AI供應鏈受惠，評價相對合理，看好台灣、日本、南韓及全亞洲科技供應鏈。
台灣 台灣居全球AI硬體與先進製程核心地位，台積電領軍帶動先進封裝、散熱、伺服器、光通訊及電力 供應鏈全面受惠。AI伺服器出貨持續增長，企業獲利與資本支出提升，科技股仍為推升台股支柱。
科技/
通訊
科技公司近期財測大多重申或提高資本支出目標，AI資料中心建設持續加速，GPU、網通、光通訊、
儲存設備需求維持強勁。AI半導體仍處獲利成長速度快於估值擴張階段，維持科技股偏多看法。
原物料
能源
資料中心建設持續，電力、能源及基建需求快速成長。AI伺服器功耗提升，天然氣、核能與再生能
源資本支出增加，銅、電力設備、儲能材料及能源供應鏈受惠，帶來的新一輪原物料需求循環。

工業 再工業化與製造業回流持續推進，AI資料中心建設帶動電力設備、變壓器、冷卻系統、自動化設備 及工程服務需求。工業企業訂單能見度提升，基建與能源轉型投資成為中長期成長動能。
債券
市場 投等債 美國投等債殖利率於9月底突破6%整數關卡且為2023年12月以來新高。雖美債殖利率居高不下， 對投等債將帶來壓力，惟可以核心資產角度持有並透過長期息收累積報酬，並以金融、科技債為主。
匯率
市場 美元 美國經濟成長與企業獲利優於多數成熟市場，聯準會維持相對偏高利率，加上歐洲主權風險升溫及 地緣政治不確定性增加，帶動全球避險資金流向美元。

21
資產別 市場 評論
股票
市場
歐洲 歐洲面臨天然氣價上升、能源成本增加、政治不確定性升溫及經濟成長落後美亞等挑戰。法國財 政赤字與債務疑慮使法德10年期公債利差擴大至2012年歐債危機以來高位，同步衝擊歐股信心。
中國 今年3Q，市場對政策出台提振經濟的期待較低，多少壓制了市場的風險偏好。10月起A股將進入 業績驗證期，業績增長確定性高的行業類股有望獲得資金青睞，預計科技股維持高增長。
新興
市場
新興市場續吸引國際資金流入。配置上，台灣與南韓受惠於AI伺服器、半導體、HBM及先進封裝
需求成長，掌握AI硬體供應鏈核心地位。
健護
醫療
醫療保健具備高度韌性且目前估值相對偏低。近期受惠於新藥發展（如減重藥等生醫創新）、AI
醫療應用普及以及企業併購活動增加，成為震盪市況中的首選防禦型配置。
公用
事業
公用事業類股因數據中心帶來的龐大電力需求，已從傳統的防禦型「收息股」轉型為兼具穩健配
息與成長潛力的標的。這類公司具備強大護城河，但高利率環境估值壓力較大。
金融 利率維持相對高檔，有利銀行淨利息收入表現，IPO與企業併購增加，也有助投銀及財管業務。

房地產 美國房地產市場呈現明顯分化趨勢。受益於股市上漲帶來的財富效應，高收入群體購房需求仍保 持相對穩健，而中低收入購房者則持續退出市場。不過，市場供給增加，買方議價能力已有改善。
消費 美國就業與薪資持續溫和成長支撐零售銷售動能，但能源漲價與生活成本攀升，家庭消費趨於保 守。消費呈兩極化：必需性消費因抗通膨而具防禦力，非必需消費因高利率壓抑貸款購買而受限。
債券
市場
美國
公債
美國財政部雖增加回購長天期債券，但回購規模相對美債市值杯水車薪，且在進入升息循環且財
政赤字擔憂延續下，美債期限溢價上升，殖利率居高不下。
非投等債 高利率環境壓力已對非投等債企業體質造成傷害，違約風險攀升，短期建議暫時避開。
新興債 目前市場願意給予高風險新興國家較低風險溢價，但未來若全球流動性收緊，資金可能快速撤離 弱勢國家。新興市場債維持中立。主因在新興市場美元債發行量增加，未來利差略有擴大風險。

Document 712: 环球时报社评：中俄美元首即将会晤，释放强烈政治信号

Source PDF: all report pdf/环球时报社评：中俄美元首即将会晤，释放强烈政治信号.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

推动构建新型⼤国关系，标志着中国特⾊⼤国外交进⼊了主动引领、系统建构的新境界。
会晤之所以得以在深圳举⾏，正是国际社会⾼度认可中国稳定、负责任⼤国形象的体现。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 19 ⽇，国家主席习近平将同俄罗斯总统普京、美国总统特朗普在深圳举⾏中俄美元⾸会
晤。毫⽆疑问，这场被国际舆论⼴泛称作 “ 史⽆前例 ” 的中俄美元⾸会晤，将成为 2026 年国际政治
最具分量的画⾯之⼀。三国通过相互协调稳定⼤国关系的努⼒，不仅是对 “ ⼤国必然⾛向对抗 ” 旧
叙事的有⼒回应，更进⼀步向动荡世界释放出明确信号：国际社会完全可以通过对话沟通，为
多极化时代找到⼀条新时期⼤国和平共处的新路。
今天的世界，没有任何全球性挑战能够绕开⼤国的参与⽽单独解决。全球产业链供应链的稳定
离不开核⼼经济体的政策协调，地区冲突的降温最终要靠有影响⼒⼤国的共同协作来有效实现
劝和促谈；全球发展、粮⻝安全、能源安全这类跨国议题，若缺少⼤国间的基本默契，很容易
陷⼊议⽽不决的僵局；⽓候治理、公共卫⽣合作、⼈⼯智能全球治理等新兴议题，若没有⼤国

协同引领，也难以形成具备普遍约束⼒的全球治理规则。中俄美都是联合国安理会常任理事
国，在国际事务中地位与分量举⾜轻重，三国共同肩负的全球治理责任，更直接关乎全⼈类的
共同福祉。
中俄美三国坐下来⾯对⾯谈，再次证明了元⾸外交不可替代的战略引领作⽤。推动构建新型⼤
国关系，标志着中国特⾊⼤国外交进⼊了主动引领、系统建构的新境界。会晤之所以得以在深
圳举⾏，正是国际社会⾼度认可中国稳定、负责任⼤国形象的体现。在元⾸外交的战略引领
下，中俄关系⻓期、健康、稳定、⾼质量发展，中美、美俄关系也开启良性互动，各⽅培育互
信、管控竞争的共识不断累积，成为三边会晤得以举⾏的⼀个重要前提。
三国元⾸即将⾯对⾯就核⼼议题交换意⻅，不仅有利于进⼀步增进⼤国间的战略互信，也向国
际社会释放出⼤国关系稳定的强烈政治信号，提振国际社会对世界发展的信⼼。在最稳定、最
负责任的中国主场，稳定、共识和信⼼⼀时⻬备，天时、地利与⼈和⽔到渠成。
站在更⻓远的时间维度看，中俄美若⾛通和平共进的新路，影响将是深远的。全球有近 200 个主

权国家，绝⼤多数国家都希望拥有⼀个稳定的外部发展环境，都能从⼤国协调带来的和平红利
中受益，⽽不想在⼤国之间选边站。当中俄美⽤实际⾏动证明新型⼤国关系是可以落地的相处
模式时，更多国家就会愿意加⼊多边合作的⾏列中来，那种靠阵营对⽴划分世界的旧思维，⾃
然会慢慢失去市场。
在⼆战结束、联合国诞⽣ 80 余年后，⼈类社会对于战争与和平、发展与安全等重⼤问题仍在苦
苦求解，但⽆论何种答案，都应当承认在⼤国关系稳定的基础上促进⼤国关系的良性互动和总
体协调，就有利于缓解纷争、抑制动乱并促进合作。从历史经验和现实需求出发，中⽅反对 “ ⼤
国共治 ” 但主张加强⼤国协调和合作；从改⾰完善全球治理和构建⼈类命运共同体的⽬标出发，
中⽅倡导平等有序的世界多极化，坚持的底⾊是国际关系⺠主化。那些认为中俄美三边会晤是
⼤国在 “ 划分新的势⼒范围 ” 、搞 “ 新雅尔塔体系 ” 的误读，显然是只看到了⽚⾯的历史⽽忽视了历
史的规律，只注重局部个体的需求⽽罔顾整体全⾯的利益，只强调了⼤国的权⼒⼀⾯⽽忽略了
⼤国应尽的责任，只忧⼼于国际格局极化的现实⽽失去了对国际关系⺠主化的理想。

中俄美三边会晤难以在⼀⼣之间解决国际社会⾯临的⼀切挑战，但它⽆疑正在打开⼀个朝向更
积极、更合理和更可持续⽅向发展的通道，同时也为⼤国之间 “ 如何相处 ” 这⼀根本性命题，提供
了⼀个值得期待的⽅向。历史的⾛向常常取决于关键节点的选择。让⼆战胜利后⾸次实现的中
俄美元⾸会晤，成为新型⼤国关系的新起点，也是全世界的共同期待。

元。

研究員/牛頓 newton@uni-psg.com

Oracle於10月Oracle AI World大會與投資者日期間，重申FY2027營
收900億美元、EPS 8.10美元，以及FY2030營收2250億美元、EPS 21
美元財測，同時揭露算力建置規劃，至FY2028將由FY2026的3GW增至
10GW，且自由現金流於FY2H28期間轉正，FY2029自由現金流達300億
美元水準，且其後將持續以自有資金維持投資擴張，而在達成FY2030
目標前，資金缺口僅200億美元，隨後即照計畫以100億美元股權和
100億美元債券完成籌資。面對市場質疑，Oracle表示透過自籌、供
應鏈融資、客戶自備硬體方式搭配，使得資金缺口得以控制。此外，
對於外界擔心的建置阻礙，則可藉由多處興建中的資料中心互相調
配伺服器建置，針對舊世代GPU續約狀況也表示需求良好，且有20%
溢價。面對已經發生的記憶體等成本高漲，抑或是對於未來能源和
廠址成本，Oracle表示其合約是以保護毛利率為基礎設計，更高的
成本雖然會擴大資金消耗，但也會帶來更高的營收和毛利，使得財
測達成的可能性更為提高，因此本次雖未調整財測數字，但補上「+」
符號表示對於目前的財測信心十足，有望超預期達成財測。在需求
未出現戲劇性翻轉的前提下，加上成本漲價的順風，仍正面看待
Oracle能完成其財測目標。預估方面，由於Oracle未揭露自籌、供
應商融資、客戶自備硬體的配比，因此較難以在財測的基礎之上，
又針對營收獲利作微調，暫時仍先維持原預估，僅就股權稀釋調整，
預估FY2027營收906.6億美元(+35% YoY)，EPS 8.44美元(+11% YoY)，
未來四季EPS為9.23美元；評價維持35倍Fwd P/E，目標價由328美元
調整至323美元，維持買進投資建議。

重點摘要：

1. Oracle為資料庫、企業軟體、IaaS綜合供應商 
Oracle(甲骨文)成立於1977年，總部位於美國德州，產品包括資料
庫、企業軟體、基礎設施雲等。資料庫包含Oracle資料庫、MySQL資
料庫，近期以Oracle自主資料庫(Oracle Autonomous Database)為主
要發展方向；企業軟體主要包括Fusion和NetSuite兩套ERP系統，及
近期併購的醫療軟體Cerner等；基礎設施雲在Oracle推出Gen 2 OCI
後大獲成功，為目前成長最快的業務。此外，Oracle於Tiktok US自
字節跳動剝離後入股(持有15%並進入董事會)，採權益法認列損益貢
獻。

### Page 2

2

Oracle已從單純的資料庫廠商，進化為橫跨基礎設施雲、多雲資料
庫、企業應用的AI全方位供應商，基礎設施雲正將龐大剩餘履約價值
轉化為實際營收，從「簽約」走向「兌現」。FY1Q27營收分布為IaaS 
38%、SaaS 22%、雲授權和本地授權3%，授權支援26%、硬體4%、服務
7%；區域分布：美洲71%、歐非中東19%、亞太10%。

圖 1: Oracle FY1Q27 營收分布

資料來源：公司公布，統一投顧整理

2. 投資者日揭露最終融資與自由現金流轉正時程 
Oracle在年度大會Oracle AI World的尾聲，於10/28舉辦投資者日，
重申FY2027(2027/05) 營收900 億美元、EPS 8.10 美元，以及
FY2030(2030/05)營收2250億美元、EPS 21美元的財測，並以「+」符
號表示對於目前的財測信心十足，有望超預期達成財測。不僅如此，
除了賣長期的餅之外，Oracle在本次投資者日追加放出中期能看的到
吃的到的現金流目標。Oracle預期資本支出高峰將落在FY2027及
FY2028，自由現金流轉正的時機點會落在FY2H28，至FY2029隨資本支
出開始下降，FY2029自由現金流預估將逾300億美元，FY2030以自有
資金和現金流滿足擴張投資需求。

圖 2: Oracle 長期財測

資料來源：公司公布

### Page 3

3

在算力建置規劃方面，截至FY2026(2026/05)累積總算力約為3GW，而
FY1Q27(2026/08)單季交付850MW，推算累積總算力約增至3.85GW，
Oracle規劃FY2027-FY2028將為資本支出高峰，總新增算力7GW，至
FY2028(202/05)累積總算力達到10GW，若將7GW平均8個季度分配，平
均每季新增875MW，與FY1Q27交付的850MW水準相當。不過，Oracle
財測FY2027資本支出上限950億美元，扣除FY1Q27約285億美元，
FY2Q27-FY4Q27平均單季資本支出約220億美元，同時，由於自由現金
流轉正時機點落在FY2H28，因此推測整體資本支出金額高峰落在
FY2Q28，以285億美元對應850MW推算，FY2028資本支出1400億美元
(+47% YoY)，而接近CY2027的四個會計年度FY3Q27-FY2Q28資本支出
由於基期FY2Q27尚不確定，但推測年增率約為46%。

圖 3: Oracle 算力建置規劃

資料來源：公司公布

除了現金流外，在市場高度關注與擔憂的籌資部分，Oracle也給出回
應，針對目前直至FY2030的財務規劃，透過自籌、供應鏈融資、客戶
自備硬體等綜合搭配，僅需額外籌資200億美元；剩餘部分，除了維
持既有債務規模外，不需要額外籌資投入資本支出。儘管Oracle投資
者日(10/28)隔週一(11/2)即宣布如計畫籌資股權100億美元和債權
100億美元，籌資需求的速度使得市場短暫受到驚訝，但實際募資順
利，兩天後(11/4)即宣布完成交易，CFO Hilary Maxson於11/11接受
媒體電視台採訪也透露認購倍數的狀況健康，因此順利快速完成募
資。

### Page 4

4

3. 算力配置具彈性，舊GPU新約價更高 
Oracle 於Oracle AI World 大 會 期 間 ， 宣 布 與Nvidia(NVDA.US) 、
Intel(INTC.US)、Google(GOOGL.US)、Cerebras(CNRS.US)等合作協
議，Oracle OCI將於2027年開始部屬Google TPU，並成為Google以外
首家提供TPU的超大規模雲端服務供應商；Cerebras推理服務也同樣
計畫於2027年推出，Oracle雖然沒有開發自研ASIC晶片，但這樣的設
定反而有助於其海納百川，以多元晶片部屬策略滿足客戶需求，即便
未來Nvidia的GPU世代落後，相關的替代採購路徑也已經打通。

針對新墨西哥州的建置，Oracle則以側面回應，表示其FY1Q27交付的
算力橫跨11國38個資料中心，而其中一個為GW級(推測為Abilene, 
Texas)，各資料中心之間伺服器安裝具備彈性，若A區域進度稍微落
後，仍可將伺服器先移至B區域和C區域安裝，資料中心建置也不是單
一園區、單一日期的一次性押注。Oracle以分棟、分機房方式逐步啟
用，資本支出與營收隨進度攀升，若工程延後，多數成本亦會遞延，
且大量硬體可在不同廠址間調度，影響性將因為規模帶來的彈性部分
抵銷。

圖 4: Oracle 資料中心交付分散程度

資料來源：公司公布

### Page 5

5

此外，針對11/7媒體傳出Microsoft(MSFT.US)先前為Bing AI算力需
求與Oracle簽訂的OCI算力採購合約將不續約，逐步終止租賃協議。
推測主要是由於Microsoft近期自研ASIC Maia 200已逐步投入，且
Microsoft將自研ASIC的主要服務對象設定為自家產品與服務，也就
是包含Bing AI在內，然Microsoft自家產品與服務的發展並非落在公
司預估的樂觀情境，使得原先對外取得的算力冗餘的必要性降低。
Oracle發言人回應媒體(Reuters)表示，市場對OCI的AI基礎設施需求
仍強勁，包括對於較早世代的GPU需求。而正好在10/28的投資者日
中，也特別針對這點有相關說明，在FY1Q27期間，較早世代的GPU的
續約與售出具有20%溢價，因此，可以從價格上揚應證Oracle表示AI
基礎設施需求仍強勁的說法。

圖 5: Oracle FY1Q27 舊款 GPU 續約溢價

資料來源：公司公布

4. 資金與金流疑慮收斂，焦點回歸交付與兌現 
綜合上述，Oracle藉Oracle AI World與投資者日回應市場擔憂，提
供現金流轉正時機與金額，且隨即順利無阻礙的完成最後的融資需
求，並解釋項目推動上的營運彈性和目前市場報價狀況，同時揭露其
多元算力設備的布局計劃。針對FY2030財務目標，雖然未提供明確上
修數字，但基本定調原財測數字將是保底目標，支撐其樂觀看法的一
點在於，Oracle與客戶之間的協議是以保底毛利率的模式鎖定利潤
率，而近期元件成本的高漲，儘管墊高資金需求和成本，但在鎖定毛
利率的保護之下，將貢獻更高的營收和毛利，又或者客戶可以自備硬
體的方式，儘管營收規模會縮小，但毛利率仍維持或更高，因此在兩
者中和之下，Oracle評估營收獲利規模超過原財測的可能性高。

### Page 6

6

圖 6: Oracle 三種模式下建置資金成本與利潤率示意

資料來源：公司公布

隨著Oracle降評後信用評等機構已明示預期Oracle近年仍將維持投
資級，加上Oracle又釋出現金流與最末筆專案籌資順利，近期可以
感受出市場已經將攻擊Oracle資金風險的敘事轉移至更小型的基礎
設施雲競爭對手。儘管仍有部分媒體緊咬不放，改放大檢視建置環
節，但截至目前Oracle仍走在其提出的財測路徑上。在需求未出現
戲劇性翻轉的前提下，加上成本漲價的順風，仍正面看待Oracle能
完成其財測目標。預估方面，由於Oracle未揭露自籌、供應商融資、
客戶自備硬體的配比，因此較難以在財測的基礎之上，又針對營收
獲利作微調，暫時仍先維持原預估，僅就股權稀釋調整，預估FY2027
營收906.6億美元(+35% YoY)，EPS 8.44美元(+11% YoY)，未來四季
EPS為9.23美元；評價維持35倍Fwd P/E，目標價由328美元調整至323
美元，維持買進投資建議。

### Page 1

涉及甲⻣⽂旗下新墨西哥、威斯康星、得克萨斯三州项⽬。新墨西哥项⽬因电⼒许可证延
误，预计折价⾄⾯值 85 美分；其余项⽬仍在 90 美分以上。核⼼⽭盾在于对少数科技巨头集
中敞⼝过⾼，英欧央⾏已同步启动监管审查，部分银⾏甚⾄退出了 Anthropic 融资交易。
摘要：
随着银⾏业对 AI 基础设施领域的集中⻛险敞⼝⽇益警惕，部分机构已开始主动出清相关贷款资
产。ING 银⾏和法国兴业银⾏近期合计挂牌出售最多 8 亿美元的数据中⼼债务，涉及多个与甲⻣
⽂旗下项⽬相关的贷款，标志着银⾏业在 AI 热潮中积累的⻛险正加速向市场释放。
据英国《⾦融时报》 11 ⽉ 17 ⽇报道，知情⼈⼠透露，上述贷款出售⾏动已持续数⽉，但美国中
期选举后⺠主党⼤获全胜，市场对美国数据中⼼监管收紧的担忧加剧，促使银⾏加快在年底前
清理资产负债表的步伐。与此同时，英国央⾏和欧洲央⾏也相继启动对银⾏数据中⼼贷款敞⼝
的审查，监管压⼒从两侧同步收紧。
从市场定价来看，部分贷款报价仍在⾯值 90 美分以上，显⽰信⽤质量整体尚可；但涉及电⼒供

应受阻的新墨西哥州项⽬，预计成交价将跌⾄⾯值 85 美分左右，反映出个别项⽬的实质性⻛
险。
ING 和法兴挂牌出售，涉及多个 Oracle 旗下项⽬
据英国《⾦融时报》报道，知情⼈⼠称，ING 银⾏正在进⾏的拍卖涉及约 5 亿美元贷款，借款⽅
包括数据中⼼运营商 CyrusOne 、 EdgeConnex 和 Vantage Data Centres ，以及甲⻣⽂在新墨西
哥州⽀持的 Project Jupiter 。其中， Project Jupiter ⽬前正⾯临电⼒许可证审批延误，甲⻣⽂已
就此发出不可抗⼒通知。
法国兴业银⾏则寻求出售约 3 亿美元的项⽬融资债务，涉及甲⻣⽂在威斯康星州的 Project
Lighthouse 和得克萨斯州的 Project Frontier 。上述三个项⽬去年合计从数⼗家银⾏筹集了逾560
亿美元的建设债务。
从定价来看，威斯康星州和得克萨斯州项⽬的相关贷款报价在⾯值90 美分以上，知情⼈⼠表
⽰，此次出售更多属于 “ 投资组合管理操作 ” ，⽽⾮对信⽤质量的急迫担忧。然⽽，新墨西哥州园

区的贷款预计将在⾯值85 美分左右定价，反映出该项⽬因电⼒许可证延误带来的额外不确定
性。
集中⻛险敞⼝触发警报，银⾏多管⻬下寻求出路
据报道，知情⼈⼠表⽰，银⾏业对 AI 数据中⼼债务的谨慎情绪已积累数⽉，核⼼⽭盾在于对少数
⼤型科技公司和前沿 AI 实验室的集中⻛险敞⼝过⾼。
Nuveen 全球能源基础设施信贷主管 Don Dimitrievich 表⽰：
他同时指出，近期债务出售提供了有利的融资条款和更具吸引⼒的回报，原因在于部分贷款机
构的⻛险重新定价以及监管资本约束。
“ 我们所谈论的资本规模，按任何标准衡量都是历史性的，已经突破了传统资本来源的极限。许
多⾦融机构的⾸席⻛险官基本上已明确表⽰，这些投资组合需要压缩。 ”

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂

81 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
律师事务所 Linklaters 纽约结构化融资合伙⼈ David Lucking 表⽰：
除直接出售贷款外，银⾏还探索了信⽤保险和⻛险转移交易以扩⼤放贷空间。近期，越来越多
的银⾏将交易结构设计为144A 格式，以便将债务出售给更⼴泛的机构投资者群体。
144A 债券是指依据美国《 1933 年证券法》第 144A 条（ Rule 144A ）规定所发⾏的私募债券。该
规则允许发⾏⼈在美国境内向合格机构投资者进⾏定向私募发⾏，⽽⽆需向美国证券交易委员
会（ SEC ）进⾏繁琐的公开注册程序。
据知情⼈⼠透露，Anthropic 的 630 亿美元债务融资包以及SpaceX ⽤于购买 AI 芯⽚的 400 亿美元
融资，均计划在未来数⽉内进⼊ 144A 债券市场交易。

值得注意的是，部分主要贷款机构已退出 Anthropic 融资交易，原因正是对该 AI 实验室集中⻛险
敞⼝的顾虑。这⼀动向进⼀步印证了银⾏业在 AI 领域⻛险管理上的整体收紧趋势。
保密条款制造对冲障碍，监管审查同步升级
在⻛险管理层⾯，银⾏⾯临⼀个新的操作难题：部分数据中⼼贷款合同中含有保密条款，禁⽌
贷款⽅参与需要共享贷款信息的第三⽅交易，包括信⽤违约互换（ CDS ）和合成⻛险转移⼯
具，这使得银⾏难以通过常规衍⽣品⼯具对冲相关敞⼝。
David Lucking 表⽰： “ 如果这类条款在市场上变得更为普遍，将会带来相当⼤的问题。 ”
与此同时，欧洲监管机构也在加强审查。据报道，知情⼈⼠透露，英国央⾏正在向英国各银⾏
索取其数据中⼼项⽬贷款的详细信息，作为常规监管⼯作的⼀部分；欧洲央⾏则正在开展⼀项
监管审查，评估欧元区银⾏对数据中⼼融资的敞⼝及管理⽅式，相关计划最早于 10 ⽉底与银⾏
⾼管会议上讨论。
“ 银⾏持有这些债务的资本效率较低，⽆论从资本⻆度，还是从⻛险限额和内部资产负债表⻆

度，银⾏都需要寻找继续放贷的⽅式。 ”
